1.      Please provide an executive summary describing the nature and scope of the proposal. Additionally,
please include the following: (1) identification of all parties, (2) description of the applicant and it’s licensure
track record, (3) the type of transaction proposed including description of the transaction and relevant costs,
(4) summary of all transfer documents, (5) summary of the organizational structure of the applicant and its
affiliates, and (6) whether the facility will be accredited.

83 Corona Street Eden Operations LLC, a newly formed entity wholly owned by Mr. Louis “Josh”
Gellis (the “Applicant”) seeks approval for the acquisition of Sunny View Nursing Home, Inc., a
licensed 57 bed skilled nursing facility located at 83 Corona Street, Warwick, Rhode Island (the
“Facility”). Pursuant to the terms of an Asset Purchase Agreement (“APA”) dated August 4, 2021,
(and amended November 3, 2021) by and among 4000 Post Road Eden Realty, LLC, 83 Corona
Street Eden Realty LLC, 73 Corona Street Eden Realty, LLC, 4040 Post Road Eden Realty, LLC
and 69 Frey Street Eden Realty, LLC (as purchasers), and 4000 Post Road LLC P&R Corona
Realty, LLC, 4040 Post Road LLC and AGIM LLC(as sellers), and an Operations Transfer
Agreement dated August 4, 2021,(and amended November 3, 2021) by and among Sunny View
Nursing, Inc., Brentwood Health Center, Ltd., Brentwood By The Bay, Ltd., as Current Operators
and 4000 Post Road Eden Operations LLC, 83 Corona Street Eden Operations, LLC, and 4040 Post
Road Eden Operations, LLC, each a Rhode Island limited liability company as New Operators, all
of the Facility’s assets including its real estate and operating assets will be conveyed to 83 Corona
Street Eden Realty LLC (the “Property Purchaser”) and 83 Corona Street Eden Operations, LLC ,
respectively (the “Proposed Transaction”). A copy of the agreements are attached at Tab 14. 83
Corona Street Eden Realty, LLC will then lease the Facility real estate to the Applicant, see Tab 24.
In the Proposed Transaction, the Property Purchaser will close on the Facility real estate on or
before December 31, 2021. The Applicant will not close on the operations of the Facility until the
receipt of final Change in Effective Control approval. Following the closing of the Facility real
estate, the Applicant (through an affiliate) will serve as manager of the Facility pursuant to a
management agreement with the current operator effective January 1, 2022, see Tab 1. As the
manager, Applicant will not have any authority to hire or fire the Facility’s chief executive officer,
maintain and control the books and records of the Facility, dispose of assets or incur liabilities on
behalf of the Facility, or adopt or enforce policies regarding the operation of the Facility

The Total purchase price for the acquisition of the operations and real estate of the Facility is
$6,270,000 ($4,827,900 attributable to the real estate and $1,442,100 attributable to the operations
and other assets.) The Applicant anticipates it will fund the purchase price as follows: (1)
$1,254,000 from the Applicant’s cash on hand which will represent a 20% equity position and (2)
$5,016,000 in the form of a loan which will represent an 80% debt position. On the closing date for
the Facility real estate the purchase price for all the purchased assets included in the APA, will be
paid subject to an escrow of $1,935,450 to be released upon closing of the operations.

Mr. Gellis has a proven track record in Rhode Island as well as outside of the State in owning and
operating skilled nursing facilities. In Rhode Island, Mr. Gellis has an ownership and operator
interest in West View Nursing & Rehabilitation Center, a 120 bed duly licensed skilled nursing
facility in West Warwick, Rhode Island, Cortland Place Rehab Center LLC, d/b/a Stillwater
Assisted Living and Skilled Nursing Community a 80 bed duly licensed skilled nursing facility in
Greenville, Rhode Island, and Overlook Nursing and Rehabilitation Center, a 100 bed duly licensed
skilled nursing facility in Pascoag, Rhode Island. Mr. Gellis also has ownership interests in Delmar
Nursing & Rehabilitation in Delmar, Delaware and Rosewood Rehabilitation and Nursing

Center in Rensselaer, New York. All of the facilities are in good standing and are proud of the quality
services provided to all residents.

December 31, 2021


11. Please provide a concise description of the services currently offered by the licensed entity and
identify any services that will be added, terminated, expanded, or reduced and state the reasons therefore:

The Facility current offers skilled nursing, physical therapy, occupational therapy and speech and
language therapy services. Included in such services is post-surgical care, medication management
and monitoring, IV therapy, diabetes care, assistance with personal hygiene and the activities of
daily living. The Facility also offers an array of social activities for its residents. It is not
anticipated that any services will be added, terminated, expanded, or removed.


12. Please identify the long-term plans of the applicant with respect to the health care programs
and health care services to be provided at the facility:

The Applicant will continue the quality services currently provided at the Facility. The
Applicant will also continue making any needed upgrades to the physical plant.

15. Please provide organization charts of both agencies (existing entity and the applicant) for prior to
transfer and post transfer, identifying all "parent" legal entities with direct or indirect ownership in or control,
all "sister" legal entities also owned or controlled by the parent(s), and all "subsidiary" legal entities.

See Tab 15 for pre-transfer and post-transfer organizational charts.

16. If the proposed owner, operator or director owned, operated or directed a health care facility (both
within and outside Rhode Island) within the past three years, please demonstrate the record of that
person(s) with respect to access of traditionally underserved populations to its health care facilities.

Nursing facilities managed and/or owned by the Applicant’s owner have an excellent track
record with respect to access to traditionally underserved populations. In particular, skilled
nursing facilities in Rhode Island (West View Stillwater and Overlook), New York and Delaware
provide charity care services to residents qualified under applicable regulations and policies and
also provide services to Medicaid and Medicare residents.

17. Please identify the proposed immediate and long-term plans of the applicant to ensure adequate
and appropriate access to the program and health care services to be provided by the health care
facility/ies to traditionally underserved populations.

The Applicant will provide charity care pursuant to its Charity Care Policy (See Tab 18). In
addition, the Applicant will comply with all non-discrimination policies and procedures required
for Medicare certification, including, without limitation, those pertaining to residents with
limited English proficiency. Lastly the Applicant will provide services to both Medicare and
Medicaid residents.

18. Please provide a copy of charity care policies and procedures and charity care application form.

See Tab 18

21. Please identify any planned actions of the applicant to reduce, limit, or contain health care costs and
improve the efficiency with which health care services are delivered to the citizens of this state.

The Applicant is planning on reducing expenses by joining a General Purchasing Organization
and obtaining better contracts because of the volume of business.

22. Please provide a copy of the Quality Assurance Policies (for the services) and a detailed explanation
of how quality assurance for patient services will be implemented at the facility/ies by the applicant.

As set forth therein, the Quality Assessment and Performance Improvement Plan outlines a
standard format for quality assessment and performance improvement which is proactive and
continually improves the way the facility cares for and engages residents, caregivers and other
partners. All employees participate in ongoing improvement activities in support of the
performance improvement mission. The Plan includes guiding principles, design/scope governance
and leadership feedback, data systems and monitoring performance improvement projects, system
analysis and action, root cause analysis, care planning process and the like.

See Tab 22

   B. The relative availability of funds for capital and operating needs; and

===== PAGE 9 =====
       The applicant has sufficient funds for capital and operating needs as evidenced in the financial
       projections in Appendix A and the existence of 30 days operating expenses set forth
       in Appendix C.

  C. The applicant’s financial capability.

       The Applicant will have sufficient funds available for capital and operating needs as
       evidenced by the financial projections in Appendix A, its initial capitalization, and the
       evidence of 30 days operating expenses set forth in Appendix C, to ensure financial
       stability, if necessary. The applicant’s financial capability is strong as evidence by the
       successful operation of other long term care facilities in Delaware, New York and Rhode
       Island by entities with overlapping ownership. The Applicant is a new entity, formed for
       the purpose of the transaction contemplated by this application, and therefore does not
       have prior financials. All of the above referenced factors demonstrate the Applicant’s
       financial condition and its financial capability to operate the facility.

                                                       Appendix B

                                        Rhode Island Department of Health
                                  Center for Health Systems Policy and Regulation
                                                Compliance Report

83 Corona Street Eden Operations LLC has applied for licensure as a healthcare facility in Rhode Island. As part of
the regulatory requirements to determine the character, competence and other quality related information of the
applicant, the Center for Health Systems Policy and Regulation is requesting the following information regarding the
health care facilities operated by or affiliated with the applicant, as listed on the attached sheet.

Please answer the following questions.
1.      Are the agencies/facilities currently licensed and in
        substantial compliance with all applicable codes,
        rules and regulations?                                           Yes__            No__

If the answer to #1 is “NO”, please identify the facility(ies) and briefly explain the licensure status.

2.      Has there been any enforcement actions against
        these agencies/facilities in the past three (3) years?           Yes__            No__

If the answer to #2 is “YES”, please identify the facility(ies) and include any information relevant to those enforcement
actions (reason for action, stipulation, fine, etc.). In addition, please furnish a brief description of the outcome of the
most recent survey, including any deficiencies cited. Additional pages may be attached, if needed.

                                             Appendix C (CONT.)

5.      Based on the format below, please provide a summary of the applicant’s administrative and operational
policies and procedures to provide individualized and resident-centered care, services, and accommodations, and a
sense of peace, safety, and community, and clearly identify how the proposal would advance these areas:

           a. Resident’s physical environment:
                   i. Accommodations for privacy vs. congregate and common areas;

                    Sunny View offers many options within the facility for accommodating their
                    privacy needs, as well as for congregating in common areas with other residents
                    and staff members. All residents have either private or semi-private rooms. Sunny
                    View offers private spaces for residents to utilize during family or other meetings

                  ii. Choice and autonomy in personal space, fixtures, furniture;

                    Whenever feasible and in the resident’s best interest, Sunny View honors patient
                    requests and supports their autonomy

                  iii. Access to and involvement in decentralized services, such as, community kitchen(s),
                       laundry, activities;

                    As set forth above, the Facility provides a wide range of activities, including
                    wellness and fitness opportunities. Laundry service is provided to residents. The
                    Facility provides a variety of dining options, including a café.

                  iv. Access to outdoors and outdoor activities (e.g., sunrooms, patios, gardens and
                      gardening);

                      Sunny View offers well maintained and quiet outdoor space for residents to
                      enjoy.

           b. Resident-centered systems of care:
                 i. Security systems and care delivery systems to foster autonomy, choice, and
                     negotiated risk;

                    Sunny View’s approach to resident-centered care is about fostering autonomy,
                    choice and negotiated risk by partnering with its residents, listening to their
                    questions, concerns, and feedback, and creating opportunities for the residents to
                    express themselves and exercise choice. Each resident received a full assessment
                    and a developed personalized care plan that recognizes their needs and
                    incorporates their wishes into their daily routine. Our security systems are in place
                    to be resident-centered based on assessment by our clinical team.




                                                   18

===== PAGE 22 =====
       ii. Individualized daily/nightly scheduling (e.g., daily rhythm, going to bed, waking);

           Each resident’s daily routine is carefully planned for as determined from his
           or her initial and periodic assessments. Meals, activities, and care are offered
           based on each individual resident’s routine.

       iii. Dining flexibility (e.g., time, access to dining style and menu choice);

        Sunny View offers meals in a variety of locations. Its main dining room offers
        restaurant-style service for three meals every day. If desired, patients may enjoy
        their meals in their rooms. Residents may choose their meal selections and when
        meals are served. Sunny View has a number of residents who are on varied meals
        schedules of their choosing.

       iv. Lifestyle/activities flexibility;

        Our residents have the opportunity to express their lifestyle choices during their
        individual assessment, as well as during their periodic assessments and care plan
        meetings, and any time in between.

c. Workforce administration:

       i. How do staffing schedules and assignments ensure consistent delivery of resident
          services and foster relationship building?

           Sunny View is staffed according to resident need. Employee assignments are
           also based on resident need and the timing of residents requiring specific
           services and care throughout the day. For direct care givers, the facility aims
           to maintain consistent staffing to specific resident locations.


       ii. Administrative status strategies for dealing with licensed staff turn-over (e.g.
           Registered nurses, Licenses Practical nurses, Nursing Assistants)

        Sunny View is constantly recruiting licensed staff to deal with inevitable turnover.
        Such recruiting occurs at nursing schools, through advertisements, and through
        informed referral networks.

MANAGEMENT AGREEMENT

This MANAGEMENT AGREEMENT (this “Agreement”) is made as of December 30,
2021 (the “Effective Date”), between SUNNY VIEW NURSING HOME, INC., a Rhode Island
corporation (“Operator”), and 83 CORONA STREET EDEN OPERATIONS LLC, a Rhode Island
limited liability company (“Manager”).

Recitals:

WHEREAS, Operator holds a license and all other permits and approvals necessary to
operate that certainS7-bed skilled nursing facility located at 83 Corona Street, Warwick, Rhode
Island 02886 and commonly known as Sunny View Nursing Home (the “Facility”);

WHEREAS, Manager is in the business of operating and managing skilled nursing care
facilities and providing operational, accounting, and financial services to such facilities;

WHEREAS, Operator and Manager are both parties to that certain Operations Transfer
Agreement dated August 4, 2021 by and between Operator and certain affiliates of Operator, and
Manager and certain affiliates of Manager, as amended by that certain Amendment to Operations
Transfer Agreement dated November 3, 2021 (collectively, the “OTA”);

WHEREAS, the OTA contemplates that effective as of the date hereof and running through
the Commencement Date (as defined in the OTA), the Facility will be managed by Manager
pursuant to this Agreement and in accordance with the terms of the OTA;

WHEREAS, in accordance with the terms of the OTA, Operator desires to engage Manager
to manage the operations of the Facility on Operator’s behalf pursuant to the terms of this
Agreement; and

WHEREAS, Manager desires to accept such engagement and provide management
services with respect to the Facility on the basis, terms and conditions set forth below.

NOW, THEREFORE, in consideration of the mutual representations, covenants and
agreements set forth below, and intending to be legally bound, Operator and Manager agree as
follows:

ARTICLE I
DEFINITIONS

1.01 Definitions. The following terms shall have the following meanings when used in
this Agreement.

“Accounting Period” means and refers to one (1) calendar month.

“Agreement” means this Management Agreement between Operator and Manager, and
any amendments, supplements, and/or restatements hereof or hereto as may be from time to time
agreed to in writing by Operator and Manager.

[OCR]
“Environmental Law(s)” means: (i) the Comprehensive Environmental Response,
Compensation and Liability Act, 42 U.S.C. Sections 9601 et seq., as now or hereafter amended
and the Resource Conservation and Recovery Act of 1976, as now or hereafter amended; (ii) the
regulations promulgated thereunder, from time to time; and (iii) all federal, state, municipal and
local laws, rules and regulations (now or hereafter in effect) dealing with the use, generation,
treatment, management, storage, disposal or abatement of Hazardous Materials or protection of
human health or the environment.

“Facility” shall have the meaning set forth in the Recitals of this Agreement.

“Facility Expenses” means those costs and expenses related to the operation, maintenance,

(b) raw food costs; (c) costs incurred to third parties under approved service contracts; (d) costs to
repair and maintain the Facility in accordance with this Agreement, including materials/equipment

taxes, Impositions and other taxes levied by Governmental Authorities; (i) phone, internet, cable
television and postage incurred by the Facility; (j) fees and other costs associated with maintaining
bank accounts for the Facility; (k) costs to rent or lease necessary Facility equipment; (1) cost
required for third-party auditors, accountants or others required in the preparation of financial
statements; (m) expenditures for furniture, fixtures and equipment at the Facility; (n) costs incurred

consulting or other expenses required to obtain and maintain licenses to operate the Facility. .

ARTICLE II
APPOINTMENT OF MANAGER

2.01 Appointment of Manager. Operator hereby appoints and engages Manager, and
Manager hereby accepts such appointment and engagement, subject to the terms and conditions of
this Agreement, to act as the sole and exclusive manager for the daily management of the Facility
during the Term of this Agreement. Except as otherwise provided herein, during the Term of this
Agreement, Manager shall have reasonable discretion in the direction, management and
supervision of the Facility, including, without limitation, to supervise, direct and control the day-
to-day business activities, management and operation, expansion, repair and renovation of the
Facility and all phases of its operations in the name of and on behalf of Operator and for Operator’s
account upon the terms and conditions hereinafter stated. Manager shall exercise the highest
degree of diligence and skill in the management of the Facility and shall exercise its reasonable
judgment in performing its services hereunder. Operator and Manager acknowledge and agree that
Operator is responsible under its license to operate the Facility and is engaging Manager by this
Agreement to carry out duties and responsibilities of Operator in connection with the operation of
the Facility, but nothing contained herein is intended to delegate to or engage Manager to provide
or perform any duties or responsibilities that are required to be provided by Operator directly under
applicable Legal Requirements. In providing services hereunder, Manager shall cause the Facility:
(i) to operate in compliance with the requirements of Governmental Authorities and third-party
payors, (ii) to provide quality care to residents, and (iii) to maintain and preserve necessary licenses,
permits and approvals. For the avoidance of doubt, Operator shall retain authority over the Facility
as required by law for a licensee of the Facility. Nothing in this Agreement shall require or obligate
Manager to perform any service or to undertake any responsibility with respect to any matter that
by law, regulation or requirement of a Governmental Authority or third-party payor is required to
be the direct responsibility or obligation of a nursing home licensee.

ARTICLE Il
MANAGEMENT FEE

3.01 Management Fee. As compensation in full for the Management Services to be
rendered by Manager hereunder, Operator shall cause the Manager to be paid a Management Fee
with respect to the Facility equal to Gross Revenues less Facility Expenses for each Accounting
Period during the Term by either: (i) directly paying Manager such monthly fee, or (ii) instructing
Manager to pay to itself, through retention or other means, such monthly fee.

ARTICLE IV
DUTIES AND RIGHTS OF MANAGER

4.01 Authority of Manager. Subject to the terms of this Agreement, and to the extent
permitted by law, the Facility shall be under the exclusive management of Manager, who shall be
responsible for the property located at and the efficient management of operations for the Facility.
Subject to the terms of this Agreement, Manager shall have discretion and control, free from
unreasonable interference in all matters relating to day-to-day management of the operation of the

===== PAGE 35 =====
[OCR]
Facility, including, without limitation, the following: fees and charges for providing
accommodations, food, services, care services, and related services to Residents and their guests;
supervision of Resident care, credit policies, food and beverage services, employment policies,
executing, modifying and terminating licenses and concessions for commercial space within the
Facility; receipt and disbursement of funds; procurement of inventories, supplies and services;
promotion and publicity; and, generally, all activities necessary for the operation of the Facility
consistent with this Agreement. Notwithstanding the foregoing, all agreements, licenses, leases
(whether of services, space or equipment, to, for, or from the Facility), concessions and
subcontracts concerning the Facility may only be entered into by Manager after they have been
approved by Operator.

4.02 Duties of Manager. As manager of the Facility, subject to the restrictions set forth
in this Agreement, Manager shall be responsible for the good faith implementation of the
management of the operation of the Facility in accordance with the terms of this Agreement and
all Legal Requirements. In addition to any other duties set forth in this Agreement but subject to
the terms of this Agreement and all Legal Requirements, Manager shall use diligent and good faith
efforts to: (a) charge and collect resident fees for providing accommodations and other services to
the Residents; (b) provide for and supervise Resident care, including applicable food, beverage,
health, transportation and other needs as determined by the Resident agreements; (c) establish
credit policies; (d) procure supplies, expendable items and other equipment necessary for
operations at the Facility; (e) enter into all Operator-approved contracts, leases and agreements
required in the ordinary course of business for the supply, operation, maintenance and service of
the Facility (including, but not limited to, food procurement, trash removal, pest control and
elevator maintenance) and pay the costs of all such services when due to the extent payment is
approved by Operator and provided that Manager shall not incur any liabilities on behalf of the
Facility; (f) market the Facility to potential Residents and referral sources; (g) interact with
appropriate regulatory staff to assure the sound management of the operation of the Facility; (h)
prepare and present to Operator the annual operating budget and capital budget for the Facility; (i)
ensure the safekeeping of the cash and other financial collateral due to Operator; (j) cooperate with
Operator to obtain, renew and maintain all licenses and permits necessary in order to manage the
Facility in accordance with Legal Requirements and the terms of this Agreement, provided that
Manager will manage under Operator’s license for the Facility and comply with the terms and
requirements of any applicable licenses and permits; (k) subject to Section 4.03 hereof, oversee,
manage and direct all day-to-day operations; (1) maintain and comply with cash controls
established by Operator from time to time; (m) comply with the terms and requirements of any
agreement regarding the Facility, including, without limitation, any lease, mortgage, loan
document or insurance policy.

4.03 Manager Restrictions. As Manager of the Facility, the Manager shall not provide
any services or perform any duties, responsibilities or obligations that may not be delegated to
Manager under applicable Legal Requirements or that are required by applicable law to be
provided directly by Operator. Manager shall be responsible for using good faith efforts to advise
Operator of such duties, responsibilities, or obligations of which it has knowledge in time sufficient
to allow such duties, responsibilities or obligations to be timely performed. In furtherance of the

===== PAGE 36 =====
[OCR]
foregoing, Manager and Operator agree and acknowledge that: (i) all personnel at the Facility,

including, without limitation, the licensed administrator of the Facility, shall be employed by
Manager during the Term, and the hiring, firing and other employment decisions with respect to
the employees at the Facility shall be exclusively determined by Manager, provided, however, that
all employment decisions regarding the licensed administrator and director of nursing of the
Facility shall be retained by Operator, and Operator shall have the exclusive right to approve of
any such employment decisions regarding such administrator and director of nursing; (ii) Operator
shall maintain and control all books and records of the Facility; (iii) Operator shall control the
disposition of assets and the incurrence of liabilities with respect to the Facility; and (iv) Operator
shall adopt and enforce all policies regarding the operation of the Facility. In addition, as part of
the management of the Facility, Manager will not: (i) replace or transfer all or any part of any of
the Facility’s units or beds to another site or location; (ii) transfer or surrender any license,
certificate, permit or rights thereunder, or demise any license, certificate, permit or rights
thereunder to any person or entity or to any location other than the Facility to which such license,

certificate or permit pertains, or pledge any license, certificate or permit as collateral security for
any indebtedness (with all licenses, certificates and permits to remain in the name of Operator, as
applicable); (iii) suffer, permit or allow to occur any rescission, withdrawal, revocation,

amendment or modification of or other alteration to the nature, tenor or scope of any license,

certificate or permit without Operator’s prior written consent, including, without limitation, any
change to the authorized units/beds capacity of the Facility and/or the number of units/beds
approved by the applicable Governmental Authority, or any change in any participation
arrangement under the Medicare or Medicaid programs; (iv) make or permit any voluntary transfer
of any Resident of the Facility, unless such transfer is at the request of the Resident (without
economic incentives being given to the Resident by Manager or an affiliate) or its payor or is for
reasons relating to non-payment or the health, required level of medical care or safety of the
Resident to be transferred; or (v) provide additional regulated services at the Facility, including,

without limitation, medical services. For the avoidance of doubt, although Manager, as part of
Manager's services pursuant to this Agreement, shall make recommendations to Operator
concerning the operation of the Facility, Operator has and shall at all times retain under this
Agreement the ultimate authority and responsibility for the operational decisions at the Facility,

and Manager shall perform the functions described in this Agreement to be performed by it in
accordance with policies and directives approved by Operator. Operator shall be the holder of all
licenses, permits and contracts obtained with respect to the Facility, and shall be the “provider”
within the meaning of all third-party contracts for the Facility. Specifically, and without limitation,
Operator also shall hold the Medicare and Medicaid provider numbers and provider agreements.

This Agreement shall not constitute an assignment (automatic or otherwise) by Operator to
Manager of the licenses, permits, contracts, certifications or provider agreements with respect to

the Facility.

4.04 Personnel Administration. Operator shall assist Manager in recruiting and
training the personnel of the Facility and in service training, attendance at seminars or conferences,
staff schedules, job descriptions and personnel policies with respect to the personnel at the Facility
in accordance with the budget for the Facility approved by Operator. At all times during the Term,


===== PAGE 37 =====
[OCR]
Manager shall ensure that all employees at the Facility continue to receive compensation and
benefits comparable to the compensation and benefits they currently enjoy on the date hereof.

4.05 Purchasing. Manager shall use, on behalf of the Facility, such purchasing systems
and procedures developed by or otherwise available to Manager for all items that are consistent
with the budget for the Facility approved by Operator. Any purchase by Manager made pursuant
to or otherwise ancillary to this Agreement shall be made by Manager, in each and every instance,
at its sole cost and expense.

4.06 Resident Agreement. Manager shall provide to Operator forms of resident
agreements, leases or other occupancy agreements it proposes to use in the Facility for any new
Residents admitted after the date hereof. Manager is hereby granted authority to execute on behalf
of Operator pre-approved forms of resident agreements, resident leases and resident occupancy
agreements with respect to a given tenancy on the forms that have been approved by Operator. All
such agreements shall comply with Legal Requirements. Notwithstanding anything to the contrary
contained herein, (i) Manager shall not enter into any agreement with any Resident that is for a
term of longer than one (1) year or that deviates in any material respect from the standard form
agreement approved by Operator without the prior written consent of Operator or that collects
more than the first and last month in advance, and (b) rates charged to Residents shall be as set
forth in the budget for the Facility approved by Operator.

4.07 Regulatory and Contractual Requirements. Manager, at its sole cost and

expense, shall cause all things to be done in and about the Facility reasonably necessary to comply
with the requirements of any applicable Legal Requirement or order of any Governmental
Authority or quasi-governmental regulatory body or agency, or board of fire underwriters
respecting the use of the Facility or the construction, maintenance, or operation thereof. Manager
shall use diligent and good faith efforts to obtain and maintain for Operator all federal, state and
local permits and licenses need for the use, operation and management of a licensed skilled nursing
facility in the State of Rhode Island. Operator agrees upon request by Manager to sign promptly
and without charge applications for licenses, permits or other instruments necessary for the use,
operation and management of the Facility in accordance with Legal Requirements and to provide
such information and perform such acts relative to the Facility as are required by law, regulation
or practice of a Governmental Authority in order for Manager to obtain for Operator any license,
permit, instrument, certificate, certification or approval with respect to the use, operation and
management of the Facility. Manager shall keep its limited liability company organization in good
standing in the State of Rhode Island and shall maintain all limited liability company permits and
licenses that Manager is required by the State of Rhode Island to maintain. For the avoidance of
doubt, to the extent possible or as required by law, all applicable licenses and permits should be
held in the name of Operator and not in the name of Manager.

4.08 Notices of Litigation and Violations. Notwithstanding anything to the contrary
contained herein, Manager shall promptly notify Operator of: (i) any alleged material breach of
any material contract or lease in effect at the Facility; (ii) any receipt by Manager of any written
or oral communication from any state or federal licensing or regulatory authorities or third-party
payor, or any written notice from any Resident or authorized representative of such Resident,

8

===== PAGE 38 =====
[OCR]
which refers to a violation of applicable law or contract, the physical condition of the Facility or
the quality of care rendered therein or that imposes or threatens impose any federal or state sanction,
including, but not limited to, any revocation, limitation or suspension of any license, ban on
admission, increased staffing mandate, escrow account intermediate sanction, receivership, or
termination of participation in, exclusion from, or supervision of payments from, any third-party
payor; (iii) any service or attempted service of any legal process upon Manager in connection with
the Facility; (iv) any other materially adverse event that has occurred or that is reasonably likely
to occur at the Facility; or (v) any causes of action brought against Manager or any other person
relating to the Facility upon obtaining knowledge thereof.

4.09 Equal Employment Opportunity. Without limitation of any provision set forth
herein, Manager shall abide by any and all applicable federal and/or State of Rhode Island equal

employment opportunity statutes, rules and regulations, including, without limitation, Title II of
the Civil Rights Act of 1964, the Equal Pay Act of 1963, the National Labor Relations Act, the
Fair Labor Standards Act, the Rehabilitation Act of 1983, and the Occupational Safety and Health
Act of 1970, all as may be from time to time modified or amended.

4.10 Equal Housing Opportunity. Without limitation of any provision set forth herein,
Operator and Manager shall abide by any and all applicable federal and/or State of Rhode Island
equal housing opportunity statutes, rules and regulations, all as may be from time to time modified
or amended.

ARTICLE V
OPERATING PROFITS, CREDITS AND COLLECTIONS, PROCEDURE
FOR HANDLING RECEIPTS AND OPERATING CAPITAL

5.01 Operating Profits, Manager shall be responsible for collecting all Gross Revenues,
including fees billed to Residents and for paying Facility Expenses out of funds of Manager as
agreed in the budget for the Facility approved by Operator. All Gross Revenues shall be the
exclusive property of Operator and shall be deposited promptly and directly into the Operator’s
Operating Account, with all funds relating to services provided by the Facility subsequent to the
Effective Date to be turned over to Manager within two (2) business days.

5.02 Gross Revenues Priority. In each Accounting Period, the following items shall
be paid in the following order of priority: (1) the Facility Expenses; and (2) the Management Fee.
As of the date hereof, the Manager’s Account shall be sufficiently and independently funded by
Manager so as to pay the Facility Expenses without reliance on the first installment of the
Management Fee. Notwithstanding that the amount of the Management Fee paid to Manager in
accordance with the terms of this Agreement may from time to time be insufficient to cover all
Facility Expenses then due and owing, Manager shall nevertheless be obligated to make up for any
shortfall in the payment of Facility Expenses out of independent Manager funds not derived from
the Management Fee or the operation of the Facility. In no event during the Term shall Operator
be required for any reason to: (i) pay any Facility Expenses; or (ii) make any payments to Manager
or any other party from sources other than Gross Revenues.


===== PAGE 39 =====
[OCR]
5.03 Credits and Collections. Manager shall install credit and collection policies and
procedures, and Manager shall institute monthly billing by the F acility, and the steps necessary for
collection of accounts and monies owed to the Facility. This may include the institution of legal
proceedings in the name of Operator, Manager, and/or the Facility, to collect such accounts or to
enforce the rights of Operator or Manager as creditor under any contract in connection with the
rendering of any service or the purchase of any goods, if necessary, after Manager has used
commercially reasonable efforts to collect such accounts, or to enforce such rights without the
institution of such proceedings. Any and all reasonable costs and/or fees charged by a third party
in connection with the collections and/or enforcement set forth in this Section shall be included in
Facility Expenses.

5.04 Accounts. Manager shall cause resident fees and all other Gross Revenues to be
directly deposited in the Operator’s Operating Account. Manager shall prudently hold and disperse
funds from the Manager’s Account to ensure the ongoing operation of the Facility for Operator.
Manager is hereby authorized to pay the applicable Management Fee to itself subject to the priority
of payments in Section 5.02. Should the Manager’s Account be insufficient to satisfy the Facility’s
needs for a month, the Manager shall make up such shortfall out of its own independent funds in
accordance with Section 5.02.

ARTICLE VI
FINANCIAL RECORDS

6.01 Accounting and Financial Records, Subject to Section 4.03, Manager shall
establish and administer accounting procedures and controls for the development, preparation and

safekeeping of records and books of accounting relating to the business and financial affairs of the
Facility, including payroll, accounts receivable and accounts payable, and shall prepare monthly,
quarterly and annual financial reports (including profit and loss statements in accordance with
requirements of Operator. Such records shall be kept consistent with GAAP. Manager shall
cooperate with Operator’s and Operator’s affiliates’ independent auditors in the preparation of any
annual audit and preparation of income tax returns. Manager shall certify such reports as true and
correct to the best of its knowledge and belief.

6.02 Reports. In addition to the reports required pursuant to Section 6.01, Manager
shall keep Operator informed as to the financial status, condition, and operation of the Facility and
as to any state or local reporting requirements in connection with the licenses and permits
necessary for Manager to manage the Facility, with written reports and such other or special reports
as Manager may from time to time determine are necessary or which Operator may reasonably
request.

6.03 Access. Operator shall have the right to inspect the Facility at any time without
notice, and upon reasonable notice to Manager, at all reasonable times during the usual business
hours of Manager, to audit, examine and make copies of books of account (including electronic
copies thereof) maintained by Manager with respect to the Facility, and Operator shall have the
right, upon reasonable notice, to perform, or have performed by a third party, a clinical, physical
operational audit, inspection or review of the Facility. Such right may be exercised through any

10

===== PAGE 40 =====
[OCR]
agent or employee designated by Operator or by an independent certified public accountant
designated by Operator. Manager shall locate any and all books of account and other records
maintained by Manager with respect to the Facility, at the Facility.

ARTICLE VII
ENVIRONMENTAL MATTERS

7.01 Environmental Matters. In the event of the discovery of noncompliance with
Environmental Law related to Hazardous Materials on any portion of the Facility during the Term
of this Agreement, Manager shall promptly provide written notice to Operator. “Hazardous
Materials” shall mean and include any substance or material containing one or more of any of the
following: “hazardous material,” “hazardous waste,” “hazardous substance,” “regulated substance,”
“petroleum,” “pollutant,” “contaminant,” “mold” or “asbestos” as such terms are defined in any
applicable Environmental Law in such concentration(s) or amount(s) as may pose clean-up,
removal, monitoring or other responsibility under the Environmental Laws, as the same may be
amended from time to time, or which may present a risk of harm to residents, invitees or employees
at the Facility.

7.02 Manager’s Environmental Indemnification, Manager shall protect, indemnify,

defend and hold harmless the Indemnitee for, from and against any and all debts, liens, claims,

causes of action, administrative orders or notices, costs, fines, penalties or expenses (including,

without limitation, engineers’ fees, remediation costs, reasonable attorneys’ fees and the cost of
litigation) imposed upon, incurred by or asserted against, any Indemnitee resulting from, either

directly or indirectly, the release of Hazardous Material in violation of applicable law occurring

on or after the date hereof, or any action or failure to take action by Manager or by any affiliate,

agent, employee, or any subcontractor of Manager, in, upon or under the Facility, including the

property on which it is located and its soil and ground water in connection with or with respect to

any environmental matters. Manager shall promptly advise Operator in writing of any action,

administrative or legal proceeding or investigation as to which this indemnification may apply,

and Manager, at Manager’s expense, shall assume on behalf of Operator (and the other Indemnitees)
and conduct with due diligence and in good faith the defense thereof with counsel satisfactory to

Operator; provided, however, that and Indemnitee shall have the right, at its option, to be

represented therein by advisory counsel of its own selection and at its own expense. In the event

of failure by Manager to fully perform in accordance with this Section 7.02, Operator, at its option,

and without relieving Manager of its obligations hereunder, may so perform, but all costs and

expenses so incurred by Operator in that event shall be reimbursed by Manager to Operator.

7.03 Environmental Notices. Each party shall undertake reasonable efforts to notify
the other party concerning the presence of any material noncompliance with Environmental Laws
or the presence of Hazardous Materials on or under the Facility of which the notifying party has
knowledge; provided, however, that unless required by Legal Requirements, the parties shall
otherwise maintain such information as confidential.

ARTICLE VII
INSURANCE

8.01 Operator’s Insurance. During the Term, Manager shall obtain in Operator’s name
and at Manager’s expense: (i) comprehensive general liability insurance, protecting Operator
against claims for injury to or death of persons or damage to or destruction of property occurring
upon, in, or about the Facility and the adjoining streets (other than streets dedicated to and accepted
for maintenance by the public); such insurance to afford limits as requested by Operator from time
to time; (ii) worker’s compensation insurance with statutory and employees’ liability insurance;
and (iii) such other coverages as reasonably requested by Operator from time to time (such
insurance to be written by companies approved by Operator which are legally qualified to issue
such insurance in the State of Rhode Island and shall name Operator as insured and Manager as an
additional named insured.

8.02 Policies. The parties shall attempt to ensure that each policy referred to in Section
8.01 above shall: (i) provide that it will not be cancelled, amended, or reduced except after not less
than three (3) days written notice to Operator and Manager; and (ii) include a waiver of all rights
of subrogation against Manager, its officers, directors and shareholders, members, constituent
partners, employees, and agents.

8.03 Cooperation, Manager and Operator each shall furnish whatever information is
reasonably requested by the other for the purpose of establishing insurance coverages.

ARTICLE Ix
TERMINATION

9.01 Termination by Either Party. Either party may by giving written notice to the other party,
terminate this Agreement upon the following occurrences which shall constitute an event of default:

@ in the event of a filing by or against either party of a petition under federal or state law
pertaining to bankruptcy or insolvency or for a reorganization or other relief; and

(ii) if either party defaults in the prompt and full performance of any other of the covenants,
obligations or agreements hereunder, and fails to correct such failure within fifteen (1S) days of receipt of
written notice from the non-defaulting party of such default (unless such default cannot reasonably be cured
within fifteen (15) days, in which event such period shall be extended for an additional fifteen (15) days,
provided the defaulting party shall have commenced in good faith to cure such default within the first such
fifteen (15) day period and shall proceed with all due diligence to correct such default thereafter).

9.02  Effect_of Termination. Upon any termination of this Agreement for any reason
whatsoever, except in connection with the consummation of the transactions contemplated by the OTA: (i)
all amounts payable to Manager from Operator under this Agreement or otherwise shall be immediately
due and payable, including, without limitation, all accrued but unpaid Management Fees, and (ii) Manager,
at its sole cost and expense, shall use all due diligence and best efforts to effect a smooth and orderly
transition of the operations of the Facility to Operator.

ARTICLE X
LEGAL ACTIONS AND INDEMNITY

10.01 Legal Actions. Manager shall institute or defend, in the name of Operator (with
Operator’s sole and unfettered approval to either institute, defend or settle in the case of claims not
covered under the insurance program required by this Agreement) any legal action pertaining to
the Facility and take any actions, in the name of Operator, necessary to protest or litigate to a final
decision in any appropriate court or forum, any alleged violation, order, rule, or regulation
affecting the Facility. Manager shall give prompt notice to Operator of any such matter and keep
Operator reasonably well informed in respect thereof and Operator shall assist and cooperate with
Manager in defense or prosecution of any such action. Any and all legal actions or proceedings to
collect charges, third-party payments, rents or other incomes for Manager, Operator, or the Facility,
or to lawfully cancel, modify or terminate any lease, license, or concession agreement in the event
of breach or default thereof, or to defend any action brought against Operator, the Facility or
Manager in connection with the management of the Facility (other than to the extent Manager is
obligated to indemnify an Indemnity pursuant to Section 10.02), shall be paid out of Gross
Revenues and be treated as Facility Expenses.

10.02 Manager’s Indemnity. To the fullest extent permitted by applicable law, Manager
shall indemnify, protect, defend and hold harmless the Indemnitee for, from and against all
liabilities, claims, damages, losses, liens, causes of action, suits, judgments and expenses,
including attorneys’ fees, of any nature, kind or description of any person or entity, directly or
indirectly arising out of, caused by, or resulting from (in whole or in part): (i) the Manager Services
provided hereunder, or any part thereof, (ii) this Agreement, or (iii) any act or omission of Manager,
any consultant or subcontractor of Manager, anyone directly or indirectly employed by them, or
anyone that they control or exercise control over (collectively, the “Liabilities”). Manager shall
promptly advise Operator in writing of any action, administrative or legal proceeding or
investigation as to which this indemnification may apply, and Manager, at Manager’s expense,
shall assume on behalf of Indemnitees and conduct with due diligence and in good faith the defense
thereof with counsel satisfactory to Operator; provided, however, that any Indemnitee shall have
the right, at its option, to be represented therein by advisory counsel of its own selection and at its
own expense. In the event of failure by Manager to fully perform in accordance with this Section
10.02, Operator, at its option, and without relieving Manager of its obligations hereunder, may so
perform, but all costs and expenses so incurred by Lessee in that event shall be reimbursed by
Manager to Operator. This indemnification shall not be limited to damages, compensation or
benefits payable under insurance policies, workers’ compensation acts, disability benefit acts or
other employee benefit acts.

intended to survive the termination of this Agreement, including, but not limited to, Article L
Section 7.02, Article X, and Article XI, shall survive the termination of this Agreement.

11.08 Assignment by Manager. Manager may not assign or otherwise transfer any rights,
interests or obligations under this Agreement (including an assignment resulting by operation of
law as a result of the merger or consolidation of Manager with any other party) without the prior
written consent of Operator, which consent Operator shall be entitled to withhold in its sole and
absolute discretion. This Agreement shall be binding on and inure to the benefit of each party’s
successors and permitted assigns.

11.09 Amendment. This Agreement, together with that certain Amendment to
Operations Transfer Agreement dated November 3, 2021, between Operator and Manager, among
others, constitutes the entire agreement between Manager and Operator relating to the subject
matter of this Agreement and supersedes all prior negotiations, discussions, correspondence,
communications, understandings, and agreements between the parties relating to the subject matter
hereof. To be effective, any amendment or modification of this Agreement must be in writing and
signed by or on behalf of Manager and Operator.

11.10 Counterparts. This Agreement may be executed in counterparts, each of which
will be deemed an original, but all of which together will constitute one and the same instrument.
In making proof with respect to this Agreement, it will be necessary to produce only one copy
hereof signed by the party to be charged. Manager and Operator may deliver executed counterpart
signature pages to this Agreement by facsimile transmission, by electronic mail in .pdf form, or by
any other electronic means intended to preserve the original graphic and pictorial appearance of a
document, and such delivery will have the same effect as physical delivery of the paper document
bearing an original signature.

ASSET PURCHASE AGREEMENT

THIS ASSET PURCHASE AGREEMENT (this “Agreement” or “APA”) is made by and
between the entities set forth on Schedule A attached hereto and incorporated herein by reference,
(collectively, the “Purchaser”), and the entities set forth on Schedule B attached hereto and
incorporated herein by reference (collectively, the “Seller’), and is effective as of August 4 , 2021
(the “Effective Date”).

RECITALS

WHEREAS, Seller is the owner of a fee simple interest in those certain real properties located
in the city of Warwick, Rhode Island and as described on Schedule B;

WHEREAS, the Sunny View Nursing Home Property and the Brentwood Health Center
Property, each as defined on Schedule B, are improved with a skilled nursing facility and the
Brentwood Bay Property, as defined on Schedule B, is improved with an assisted living facility
(referred to herein from time to time collectively as the “Facilities” and each a “Facility”);

WHEREAS, the Facilities maintain a collective skilled nursing bed count of 153 beds and an
assisted living bed count of 68 units comprising 74 individual beds (collectively the “Bed Count”)
and 73 Corona Street and 69 Frey Street are improved with certain structures but not any such
Facilities;

WHEREAS, the real property described on Schedule B, including as applicable each Facility,
any other improvements located on such real property, together with all appurtenances relating
thereto, and the personal property owned by Seller and located on such real property, are collectively
referred to herein as the “Property.”

WHEREAS, Seller desires to sell the Property to Purchaser, and Purchaser desires to purchase
the Property from Seller and substantially all the assets, rights, and interests of Seller used in operation
of the Facilities in accordance with the terms and conditions contained in this Agreement;

WHEREAS, concurrently with the execution of this Agreement, the Current Operator (as
defined in Section 12 hereof) and an affiliate of Purchaser (“New Operator”) will enter into an
Operations Transfer Agreement (the “OTA”) in order to provide for an orderly transition of the
operations of the Facilities and the transfer of certain assets thereunder; and

WHEREAS, the parties wish to enter into this Agreement as provided herein. Capitalized
terms used in this Agreement and not otherwise defined herein shall have the respective meanings
given to them in the OTA.

(a) As soon as practicable after the Effective Date, Seller shall deliver or cause to be

associated with the Property for the last two (2) years; (ii) any ALTA survey of the Property currently

maintained by Seller or its manager in connection with the operations, leasing, maintenance and/or

was performed, as well as what is commonly known as an Environmental walk-over assessment); and
(v) Seller’s most current title insurance policy for the Property (the “Existing Title Policy”)
(collectively, the “Due Diligence Materials”).

(b) On or before the date (the “Due Diligence Expiration Date”) that is forty-five (45)
calendar days after the Effective Date (the period up to and including the Due Diligence Expiration
Date, the “Due Diligence Period”), Purchaser shall have completed its due diligence investigation of
the Property provided that Purchaser received all Necessary Documents and Reports, including but
not limited to, any property reports under this Agreement (collectively “Necessary Documents and
Reports”). The Purchaser shall deliver copies of any such reports to Seller within five (5) business
days following receipt thereof by Purchaser. Upon a showing by Purchaser of the prompt ordering
and diligent pursuit of all third-party reports (e.g. survey, zoning, appraisals, etc.) by Purchaser, and
upon a showing by Purchaser of a delay in receiving such third-party reports due to no fault of
Purchaser based on circumstances outside of Purchaser’s control, the Due Diligence Period shall be
extended for an additional twenty (20) days. At any time at or before the expiration of the Due
Diligence Period, Purchaser shall have the right, in Purchaser’s sole and absolute discretion, to notify
Seller (which notice may be sent solely via e-mail): (i) that Purchaser has determined to proceed with
the acquisition of the Property, and in the event of such notice the Due Diligence Period will be
deemed to have expired as of the date of such notice; or (ii) of Purchaser’s termination of this
Agreement, for any reason or for no reason in its sole and absolute discretion, whereupon Purchaser
shall receive a prompt refund of the Deposit, and this Agreement shall be null and void and the parties
shall have no further obligation to each other. If Purchaser fails to give either of such notices prior to
the expiration of the Due Diligence Period, the Purchaser shall be deemed to have elected to proceed
with the acquisition of the Property and any termination right of Purchaser pursuant to this Section
6(b) shall be waived.

9. Real Estate Taxes and Assessments.

(a) The parties shall pay and allocate real estate taxes and special assessments with respect
to the Real Property as follows:

(i) At or before Closing, Seller shall pay all real estate taxes and special
assessments, and any penalties and interest thereon, if any, which were due and payable with respect
to the Real Property for all periods, time and years prior to 2021, including any deferred taxes
attributable to years prior to 2021. Any special assessments in place or incurred prior to the Effective
Closing Date shall be paid in full by Seller prior to Closing.

(ii) Any installments of real estate taxes and special assessments with respect to
the Real Property which are due and payable in 2021 shall be prorated between Seller and Purchaser
as of the Effective Closing Date. Proration shall be made on a per diem basis using a calendar year. If
the Closing shall occur before the tax rate is fixed, the apportionment of taxes shall be upon the basis
of one hundred and five (105%) percent of the tax for the next preceding year applied to the latest
assessed valuation. If subsequent to the Closing, real estate taxes (by reason of change in either
assessment or rate or for any other reason) for the Real Property should be determined to be higher or
lower than those that are apportioned, a new computation shall be made, and any amount owing
pursuant to such recomputation shall be promptly paid. The provisions of this subsection shall survive
the Closing.

10. Contingencies to Purchaser’s Obligations.

(a) Prior to Closing, there shall have been: (i) no matter affecting the Real Property that
would materially interfere with the operation of the Facilities; (ii) and no matter that would render
title to the Real Property unmarketable.

(b) Seller shall have performed its obligations under this Agreement and the warranties,
representations and covenants of Seller made in this Agreement shall be true, correct and complete as
of the date of this Agreement and as of the Effective Closing Date as though made at that time.

(c) New Operator will be able to obtain licensing approval from the Rhode Island
Department of Health for operation of each Facility at its current licensed bed capacity, which
approval Purchaser and/or New Operator shall diligently pursue. Purchaser shall be entitled to a pro

New Operator whatsoever, fewer than the current licensed beds are delivered to Purchaser and/or New
Operator at Closing.

14. Closing; Required Authorizations; Conditions Precedent.

(a) The closing of the acquisition of the Property (the “Closing”) shall occur on the date
which is the first day of the calendar month following thirty (30) days from the date on which New
Operator obtains the Required Authorizations (as defined below) (as applicable, the “Closing Date”
or “Effective Closing Date”), and shall be consummated through the mutual exchange of documents
and funds through the escrow established with the Title Company. The “Effective Closing Date” for
purposes of this Agreement shall be the actual date the Closing is held, unless otherwise stipulated in
writing by the parties.

(b) Purchaser and Seller hereby agree that the Operations Transfer shall occur under the
terms of the OTA as of 12:00:01 a.m. on the Closing Date, and that the Closing shall be deemed to
have occurred concurrently therewith but the disbursement of funds and the release of closing
documents by the Title Company hereunder shall not occur until the opening of business on the
Closing Date, if the Closing Date is a business day, or the first (1st) business day following the Closing
Date, if the Closing Date is not a business day (as applicable, the “Disbursement Date’). To
effectuate the same, no later than the business day immediately prior to the Closing Date, each of
Seller and Purchaser shall send written authorization to the Title Company authorizing the
disbursement of funds and the release of all closing documents as of the opening of business on the
Disbursement Date, and such authorizations shall be deemed irrevocable and unconditional on the
part of the issuing party and the Title Company shall be required to act in accordance with such written
authorizations as of the opening of business on the Disbursement Date. If the Operations Transfer
occurs and either party, prior to the disbursement of funds and the release of all closing documents,
takes any action to delay or prevent the disbursement of funds and the release of all closing documents
as of the opening of business on the Disbursement Date, such action shall constitute an immediate
breach of this Agreement and, notwithstanding anything to the contrary herein, including, without
limitation, Section 3 above, the breaching party shall indemnify, defend and hold harmless the non-
breaching party and its employees, affiliates, managers, shareholders, officers, directors and agents
from any loss, cost, claim, damage, cause of action, liability, expense (including reasonable attorneys’
fees and costs), fine, penalty or forfeitures arising from or relating to the failure to consummate the
Closing hereunder as of the Disbursement Date.

(c) On or prior to the Closing Date, Seller shall deposit, or cause to be deposited, the
Escrow Holdback with the Title Company as escrow agent as security for Seller’s obligations for the
purpose of satisfying any post-closing indemnification obligations of Seller under this Agreement,
pursuant to the terms of the Escrow Holdback Agreement.

(d) Purchaser hereby covenants and agrees to use its best efforts to secure the Required
Authorizations prior to the Closing Date, including, without limitation, by submitting its initial
application for the Required Authorizations (the “Required Authorization Application”) with the

Period; provided, however, that the Purchaser shall not submit the Required Authorization Application

customarily obtained from State licensing authorities by reasonable operators of facilities like the
Facility, acting in good faith, before such an operator takes possession of, and begins to operate, a

Purchaser receives permission from the applicable State licensing authorities to assume operational
control of the Facilities prior to the issuance of a non-provisional or non-conditional license for the
Facilities (e.g., due to a State licensing authority’s requirement that a survey of Purchaser’s operations
at the Facilities be completed prior to the issuance of a non-provisional or non-conditional license)
and, under local custom and practice, reasonable operators of facilities like the Facilities customarily
take possession of, and begin to operate, facilities like the Facilities on the basis of such permission,
then, for purposes of this Section, the date of such permission would be treated as the date that
Purchaser obtained the Required Authorizations, and in any such event, Purchaser will pay Seller the
Purchase Price as of such date.

16. Maintenance, Repairs, Risk of Loss.

(a) Seller shall continue to maintain and operate the Property in good repair, including,
but not limited to, repairs related to wear and tear of the Property as well as damage to the Property,
between the Due Diligence Expiration Date and the Closing in the same manner as prior to the
Execution of this Agreement. If subsequent to the Closing Purchaser discovers that Seller did not
make necessary repairs to the Property for wear and tear or damage that arose during the Due
Diligence Expiration Date and the Closing, Purchaser shall be entitled to reimbursement from Seller,
either directly or from any escrow funds, of all costs and fees, including, but not limited to reasonable
attorneys’ fees, associated with repairing the Property and obtaining payments thereof from Seller.

(b) Fire or Other Casualty. If, prior to the Closing, any individual Property upon which a
Facility is situated is damaged by fire, vandalism, acts of God or other casualty or cause, Seller shall
promptly give Purchaser notice of any such damage (the “Damage Notice”), together with Seller’s
estimate of the cost and period of repair and restoration. In any such event: (a) in the case of damage
to the individual Property of less than $150,000.00 and from a risk covered by insurance maintained
with respect to the Premises, this Agreement, including the Purchase Price, shall not be affected by
such damage and Purchaser shall take the individual Property at the Closing (or if the Facility situated
upon the individual Property is not operational at such time, then the Closing shall be postponed until
the Facility is reopened) as it is together with any applicable insurance proceeds in amounts sufficient
to repair such damages (excluding such portion of such proceeds as shall have been received by Seller,
or to which Seller shall be entitled, for loss of rentals suffered prior to the Closing Date and sums
expended for the curing of violations, emergency repairs or restorations, herein referred to as the
(“Excluded Sums”)) or the right to receive the same; or (b) in the case of either (1) damage to any
individual Property upon which a Facility is situated of $150,000.00 or more or (ii) damage to
individual Property upon which a Facility is situated from a risk not covered by insurance, Purchaser
shall have the option of (x) taking the individual Property upon which a Facility is situated at the
Closing in accordance with clause (a) above or (y) terminating this Agreement within twenty (20)
days after receipt of the Damage Notice, TIME BEING OF THE ESSENCE with respect to the time
period for the giving such termination notice, which termination shall be deemed effective if Purchaser
does not deliver written notice of its election to waive such termination within such twenty (20) day
period. If, pursuant to the preceding sentence, Purchaser is either obligated or elects to take the
individual Property upon which a Facility is situated as it is together with any applicable insurance
proceeds or the right to receive the same, Seller agrees to cooperate with Purchaser in any loss

adjustment negotiations, legal actions and agreements with the insurance company, and to assign to

any taking that (a) includes any part of the Facility building or (b) otherwise (on a permanent basis)
materially limits or materially restricts ingress and egress to and from the individual Property upon
which a Facility is situated.

this Agreement. Notwithstanding the foregoing, in the event the date for the performance of an action

day; provided, however, the foregoing shall not be applicable to the Closing, which shall occur on the

waiver shall be limited to the circumstance or event specifically referenced in the written waiver
document and shall not be deemed a waiver of any other term hereof or of the same circumstance or
event upon any recurrence thereof.

26. Headings; Defined Terms; Interpretation. The paragraph and section headings are inserted
for convenience only and are in no way intended to describe, interpret, define or limit the scope or
content of this Agreement or any provision hereof. As used herein, a “business day” shall mean a day
other than Saturday, Sunday or any day on which banking institutions in New York, New York, are
authorized by law or other governmental action to close. Wherever possible, each provision of this
Agreement shall be interpreted in such a manner as to be valid under applicable law, but, if any
provision of this Agreement shall be invalid or prohibited thereunder, such invalidity or prohibition
shall be construed as if such invalid or prohibited provision had not been inserted herein and shall not
affect the remainder of such provision or the remaining provisions of this Agreement. The language
in all parts of this Agreement shall be in all cases construed simply according to its fair meaning and
not strictly against the party that drafted such language.

27. Governing Law; Venue. This Agreement and the legal relations among the parties shall be
governed by and construed in accordance with the laws of the State of Rhode Island, without giving
effect to choice of law principles thereof. Any dispute arising out of or in connection with this
Agreement or the transactions contemplated by this Agreement shall be venued exclusively in the
state or federal courts located in the state of Rhode Island, and all parties hereby consent to such venue
and the personal jurisdiction of such courts.

28. No Third-Party Benefit. Nothing in this Agreement, express or implied, is intended to confer
on any person other than the parties to this Agreement or their permitted successors or assigns, any
rights, remedies, obligations, or liabilities under or by reason of this Agreement.

29. Severability. If any provision of this Agreement is held invalid or unenforceable or in conflict
with any applicable state, federal or local law, the validity of the remaining provisions of this
Agreement shall not be affected thereby and shall remain valid and enforceable.

30. Documents. Seller and Purchaser shall, at or after the Closing, execute, acknowledge and
deliver to each other and the Title Company such document, instrument and further assurances as
Seller, Purchaser or the Title Company may reasonably request in order to carry out the purposes of
the Agreement. This Section shall survive the Closing.

31. Dispute Resolution. The parties hereto agree that with respect to all disputes, problems or
claims arising out of or in connection with this Agreement and all other agreements or other
instruments executed in connection herewith (any of the foregoing a “Dispute”), the parties hereto
shall, in good faith, use their reasonable best efforts to resolve the Dispute. If after such efforts the
parties hereto are unable within ten (10) days of the arising of the Dispute to resolve the Dispute in
good faith, either party hereto may submit to final and binding arbitration before the American Health
Lawyers Association (the “AHLA”), or its successor, pursuant to the Federal Arbitration Act, 9
U.S.C. Sec. 1 et seq. Either party may commence the arbitration process called for in this Agreement
by filing a written demand for arbitration with the AHLA, with a copy to the other party. The
arbitration will be conducted in Warwick, Rhode Island. The parties will cooperate with the AHLA
and with one another in selecting an arbitrator from the AHLA’s panel of neutrals, and in scheduling
the arbitration proceedings. The provisions of this Section with respect to the arbitration before the
AHLA may be enforced by any court of competent jurisdiction, and the party seeking enforcement
shall be entitled to an award of all costs, fees and expenses, including attorneys’ fees, to be paid by
the parties against whom enforcement is ordered. The fees and expenses of such arbitration shall be

27

===== PAGE 76 =====
[OCR]
bome by the non-prevailing party, as determined by such arbitration. Upon the mutual agreement of
the parties involved in the Dispute, the parties may submit to final and binding arbitration before any
other recognized alternative dispute resolution company or organization. The parties hereto agree that
this Section has been included to rapidly and inexpensively resolve any disputes between them with
respect to the matters described above, and that this paragraph shall be grounds for dismissal of any
court action commenced by any party with respect to a dispute arising out of such matters.

32. Attorneys’ Fees. If either party commences an action against the other to interpret or enforce
any of the terms of this Agreement or because of the breach by the other party of any of the terms
hereof, the losing party shall pay to the prevailing party reasonable attorneys’ fees, costs and expenses
incurred in connection with the prosecution or defense of such action, whether or not the action is
prosecuted to a final judgment.

33. Successors and Assigns. This Agreement shall be binding upon and inure to the benefit of
Seller and Purchaser, and their respective successors and assigns, if any. It is specifically agreed that
Purchaser may nominate another party to take title to the Property at Closing, but no such nomination
shall relieve Purchaser of any liability hereunder. If Purchaser nominates another party to take title to
the Property, Purchaser shall, prior to the Closing, provide to Seller the federal employer identification
number for such nominee.

34, 1031 Exchange. Purchaser or Seller may consummate the purchase or sale of the Property as
part of a so-called like kind exchange (the “Exchange”) pursuant to Section 1031 of the Internal
Revenue Code of 1986, as amended (the “Code”), provided that: (a) the Closing shall not be delayed
or affected by reason of the Exchange nor shall the consummation or accomplishment of the Exchange
be a condition precedent or condition subsequent to Purchaser’s or Seller’s obligations under this
Agreement; (b) Seller shall not be required to take an assignment of the purchase agreement for the
relinquished property or be required to acquire or hold title to any real property for purposes of
consummating the Exchange; (c) a party performing an Exchange shall pay any additional costs that
would not otherwise have been incurred had such party not consummated an Exchange; (d) neither
party’s acquiescence to an Exchange shall affect or diminish in any manner its rights hereunder nor
shall the party not performing an Exchange be responsible for compliance with or be deemed to have
warranted to the other party that the Exchange in fact complies with Section 1031 of the Code; and
(e) any party performing an Exchange shall indemnify, defend, and hold harmless the other party from
or against all claims, losses, costs, damages, liabilities (including reasonable attorneys’ fees) in
connection therewith.

35. Counterparts. This Agreement may be executed in any number of counterparts, each of which
shall be deemed an original, but all of which, when taken together, shall constitute one and the same
instrument. Executed copies hereof may be delivered by facsimile, email or other electronic means
and upon receipt will be deemed originals and binding upon the parties hereto, regardless of whether
originals are delivered thereafter.

36. Confidentiality. Purchaser agrees that, (a) except as otherwise required by valid law and (b)
