EXHIBIT B
FORM OF ESCROW HOLDBACK AGREEMENT

ESCROW HOLDBACK AGREEMENT (this “Agreement”) dated as of August 4, 2021,
by and among by and among 4000 POST ROAD LLC, 4040 POST ROAD LLC, P & R CORONA
REALTY, LLC, AGIM, LLC (collectively, the “Seller”), and 4000 POST ROAD EDEN REALTY
LLC, 83 CORONA STREET EDEN REALTY LLC, 4040 POST ROAD EDEN REALTY LLC, 73
CORONA STREET EDEN REALTY LLC, 69 FREY STREET EDEN REALTY LLC (collectively,
the “Buyer”), and Landmark Abstract Agency, LLC (the “Escrow Agent”).

WITNESSETH

WHEREAS, Seller and Buyer have entered into that certain Asset Purchase Agreement dated
as of August 4, 2021 (the “APA”), which provides for, among other things, the sale by Seller and the
purchase by Buyer of certain real and personal property and such other assets, as more fully described
in the APA;

WHEREAS, pursuant to the APA, at closing, Seller shall provide the Recoupment Estimate
(together with any interest thereon, the “UHIP Escrow Fund”) to be placed into an escrow account
(the “UHIP Escrow Account”) to be maintained by Escrow Agent for the purpose of satisfying and
Pre-Closing UHIP Recoupment obligations of Seller;

WHEREAS, pursuant to the APA, Three Hundred Thousand Dollars ($300,000) (together
with any interest earned thereon, the “Indemnity Escrow Fund”, together with the UHIP Escrow
Fund, the “Escrow Funds”) is required to be placed into an escrow account (the “Indemnity Escrow
Account”, together with the UHIP Escrow Account, the “Escrow Accounts”) to be maintained by
the Escrow Agent for the purpose of satisfying any post-closing indemnification obligations of Seller
under the APA; and

WHEREAS, the parties wish to enter into this Agreement as provided under the APA.
Capitalized terms used in this Agreement and not otherwise defined herein shall have the respective
meanings given to them in the APA.

NOW, THEREFORE, it is agreed as follows:
ARTICLE I
Escrow

Section 1.01 Appointment of Escrow Agent. The parties hereto hereby appoint Landmark
Abstract Agency, LLC, and Landmark Abstract Agency, LLC hereby agrees to serve, as Escrow
Agent in accordance with, and pursuant to, this Agreement.

Section 1.02 Operation of Indemnity Escrow Account. The parties hereto agree that, subject
to Section 1.03 hereof, the Indemnity Escrow Account shall operate as follows:

35

===== PAGE 84 =====
[OCR]
@ On the Closing Date, Seller shall deposit into the Indemnity Escrow Account an
aggregate amount equal to the Indemnity Escrow Fund, to be held in an interest earning bank account
with an FDIC-insured bank of the Escrow Agent’s choice. Seller shall deposit into the UHIP Escrow
Account an aggregate amount equal to the UHIP Escrow Fund, to be held in an interest earning bank
account with an FDIC-insured bank of the Escrow Agent’s choice.

(0) Subject to Section 1.02(c) hereof, at any time commencing on the Closing Date and
continuing until the twenty four (24) month anniversary of the Closing Date, Buyer shall be entitled
to give written notice to the Escrow Agent (with a copy to Seller), to the effect that there has been an
event entitling Buyer to indemnification from Seller under the APA, which notice shall identify, in
reasonable detail, the basis therefor (the “Claim”) and estimate the amount owed by Seller pursuant
to the APA.

© On the one-year anniversary of the Closing Date (the “First Release Date”), One
Hundred Thousand Dollars ($100,000.00) of the Indemnity Escrow Fund, less the amount of any
Claim previously paid and any then-pending Claim (or any lesser amount as may then be held in
escrow by Escrow Agent) prior to the First Release Date, shall be disbursed to Seller.

@ Thirty (30) days after the Escrow Agent has received a written notice pursuant to
Section 1.02(b) (or, if not a business day, on the next business day following such thirtieth day) the
Escrow Agent shall deliver to Buyer the portion of the Indemnity Escrow Fund in the amount specified
in such notice unless Seller shall have notified the Escrow Agent (with a copy to Buyer) in writing
before such date that it disagrees with Buyer’s determination that it is entitled to indemnification with
respect to the APA, which notice shall set forth in reasonable detail the basis for such disagreement
(an “Objection Notice”). Upon the receipt of an Objection Notice within such period, the Escrow
Agent shall retain the amount of the Claim in the Indemnity Escrow Account until: (i) the rights of
Buyer, and/or Seller have been determined by final judgment of a court of competent jurisdiction and
the Escrow Agent has received evidence reasonably satisfactory to it of such final judgment, at which
time the Escrow Agent shall make the appropriate disbursement from the Indemnity Escrow Account
in accordance with such final judgment; or (ii) the Escrow Agent has received written instructions
signed by both Buyer and Seller with respect to such Claim under the APA or as to the disbursement
of all or part of the Indemnity Escrow Fund, at which time the Escrow Agent shall make the
appropriate disbursement from the Escrow Account in accordance with such written instructions. For
purposes of this Agreement, a final judgment shall be a judgment as to which the period of time for
appealing such judgment has expired without an appeal having been timely made, or, if an appeal is
timely made, as to which such appeal has been finally disposed of and there is no recourse to further
appeals.

© Should any dispute arise with respect to the delivery, ownership, or right of
possession of all or any portion of the Indemnity Escrow Fund, the Escrow Agent is authorized and
directed to retain in its possession without liability to anyone all or any part of the Indemnity Escrow
Fund until such dispute shall have been settled either by mutual agreement by the parties concerned
or by a final order, decree, or judgment of a court of competent jurisdiction in the State of Rhode
Island or the United States of America and time for appeal has expired and no appeal has been
perfected, but the Escrow Agent shall be under no duty whatsoever to institute or defend any such
proceedings, and may, in its discretion, deposit all or such portion of the Indemnity Escrow Fund with
a court of competent jurisdiction in the State of Rhode Island or the United States of America which

36

===== PAGE 85 =====
[OCR]
is hearing such dispute.

Section 1.03 Distribution of Indemnity Escrow Fund. Unless a Claim for which notice
under Section 1.02(b) has been given is then pending, the Indemnity Escrow Fund, remaining in the
Indemnity Escrow Account as of the twenty-four (24) month anniversary of the Closing Date shall be
delivered to Seller within five (5) days after such anniversary.

Section 1.04 Operation of UHIP Escrow Account. The parties hereto agree that, subject to
Section 1.05 hereof, the UHIP Escrow Account shall operate as follows:

@ On the Closing Date, Seller shall deposit into the UHIP Escrow Account an aggregate
amount equal to the UHIP Escrow Fund, to be held in an interest earning bank account of the Escrow
Agent’s choice.

(()] Subject to Section 1.5(c) hereof, at any time commencing on the Closing Date and
continuing until the twenty four (24) month anniversary of the Closing Date, upon receipt by either
Buyer or Seller, of any invoice from UHIP for a Pre-Closing UHIP Recoupment, Buyer shall be
entitled to give written notice to the Escrow Agent (with a copy to Seller), to the effect that there has
been an event entitling UHIP to a Pre-Closing UHIP Recoupment payment from the UHIP Escrow
Fund, which notice shall identify, in reasonable detail, the basis therefor (the “UHIP Claim”) and
estimate the amount owed by Seller pursuant to the APA.

© Thirty (30) days after the Escrow Agent has received a written notice pursuant to
Section 1.04(b) (or, if not a business day, on the next business day following such thirtieth day) the
Escrow Agent shall deliver to UHIP, the portion of the UHIP Escrow Fund in the amount specified
in such notice unless Seller shall have notified the Escrow Agent (with a copy to Buyer) in writing
before such date that it disagrees with Buyer or UHIP’s determination that such invoice from UHIP
is for a valid Pre-Closing UHIP Recoupment claim, which notice shall set forth in reasonable detail
the basis for such disagreement (a “UHIP Objection Notice”). Upon the receipt of a UHIP Objection
Notice within such period, the Escrow Agent shall retain the amount of the Claim in the UHIP
Escrow Account until: (i) the UHIP has provided sufficient evidence setting forth that the invoiced
amount is a Pre-Closing UHIP Recoupment, at which time the Escrow Agent shall make the
appropriate disbursement from the UHIP Escrow Account in accordance with such final judgment;
or (ii) the Escrow Agent has received written instructions signed by both Buyer and Seller with
respect to such UHIP Claim, at which time the Escrow Agent shall make the appropriate
disbursement from the UHIP Escrow Account in accordance with such written instructions.

@ Should any dispute arise with respect to the delivery, ownership, or right of
possession of all or any portion of the UHIP Escrow Fund, the Escrow Agent is authorized and
directed to retain in its possession without liability to anyone all or any part of the UHIP Escrow
Fund until such dispute shall have been settled either by mutual agreement by the parties concerned
or by a final order, decree, or judgment of a court of competent jurisdiction in the State of Rhode
Island or the United States of America and time for appeal has expired and no appeal has been
perfected, but the Escrow Agent shall be under no duty whatsoever to institute or defend any such
proceedings, and may, in its discretion, deposit all or such portion of the UHIP Escrow Fund with a
court of competent jurisdiction in the State of Rhode Island or the United States of America which
is hearing such dispute.

37

[OCR]
Section 1.05 Distribution of UHIP Escrow Fund. Unless a UHIP Claim for which notice
under Section 1.04(b) has been given is then pending, the UHIP Escrow Fund and an interest accrued
therein, remaining in the UHIP Escrow Account as of the twenty-four (24) month anniversary of the
Closing Date shall be delivered to Seller within five (5) days after such anniversary.

Section 1.06 Termination of Escrow Account. This Agreement and the Escrow Account will
terminate at 5:00 P.M., Rhode Island local time, on the date on which all of the Indemnity Escrow
Fund shall be distributed as set forth above.

ARTICLE III
Concerning the Escrow Agent

Section 3.01. Duties. To induce the Escrow Agent to act hereunder, it is further agreed by
Buyer and Seller that:

a. The Escrow Agent undertakes to perform only those duties which are expressly set
forth herein, and acknowledge that these duties are purely ministerial in nature.

b. The Escrow Agent shall be entitled to rely upon the authenticity of any signature and
the genuineness and validity of any writing received by Escrow Agent relating to this Agreement.
Escrow Agent may rely upon any oral identification of a party notifying Escrow Agent orally as to
matters relating to this Agreement if such oral notification is permitted hereunder. Escrow Agent is
not responsible for the nature, content, validity or enforceability of any of the escrow documents
except for those documents prepared by Escrow Agent.

ch The Escrow Agent shall be entitled to receive reimbursement as Escrow Agent of
documented reasonable attorneys’ fees and other documented out-of- pocket expenses incurred by it
in the performance of its duties under this Agreement, which shall be paid in equal amounts by Buyer,
on one hand, and Seller, on the other hand.

d. Ifthe Indemnity Escrow Fund is at any time attached, garnished, or levied upon under
any court order or if the payment or delivery of the Indemnity Escrow Fund is stayed or enjoined by
any court order, or if any order, judgment or decree shall be made or entered by any court affecting
the Indemnity Escrow Fund, Escrow Agent is authorized, in its sole discretion, to rely upon and
comply with the order, writ, judgment or decree. Escrow Agent shall not be liable to any of the parties
or to any other person firm or corporation by reason of such compliance even though the order, writ,
judgment or decree may be subsequently reversed modified, annulled, set aside or vacated.

e. Upon making disposition of all the Escrow Funds in accordance with this Agreement,
Escrow Agent shall be deemed fully released and discharged from any and all duties and obligations
under this Agreement, without the need that any other documentation be executed by Buyer or Seller.

f. Escrow Agent shall not be responsible for (i) any fluctuations in the interest rate
applicable to any cash held by it pursuant to or by virtue of this Agreement, or (ii) the validity,
sufficiency, collectability, or legal effect of any instrument deposited with Escrow Agent.

39

===== PAGE 88 =====
[OCR]
g. Notwithstanding anything contained in this Agreement to the contrary, Escrow Agent
has the right (but not the obligation) to require from Buyer and Seller a written release of liability of
Escrow Agent, a written authorization to disburse the Indemnity Escrow Fund, or both.

h. The parties hereto do hereby certify that they are aware that the Federal Deposit
Insurance Corporation (“FDIC”) coverages apply only to a cumulative maximum amount of
$250,000 for each individual depositor for all of depositor’s accounts at the same or related institution.
The parties hereto further understand that certain banking instruments such as, but not limited to,
repurchase agreements and letters of credit are not covered at all by FDIC insurance.

i. Further the parties hereto understand that Escrow Agent assumes no responsibility
for, nor will the parties hereto hold Escrow Agent liable for, any loss occurring which arises from the
fact that the amount of the above account may cause the aggregate amount of any individual
depositor’s accounts to exceed $250,000 and that the excess amount is not insured by the Federal
Deposit Insurance Corporation or that FDIC insurance is not available on certain types of bank
instruments.

ie The parties to this Agreement acknowledge that the maintenance of such escrow
accounts with some depository institutions may result in Escrow Agent being provided with an array
of bank services, accommodations or other benefits by the depository institution. Escrow Agent or its
affiliates also may elect to enter into other business transactions with or obtain loans for investment
or other purposes from the depository institution. All such services, accommodations and other
benefits shall accrue to Escrow Agent, and Escrow Agent shall have no obligation to account to the
parties to the escrow for the value of such services, accommodations or other benefits.

ARTICLE IV

Miscellaneous

Section 4.01 Binding Effect. This Agreement shall be binding upon, and inure solely to the
benefit of, the parties hereto and their respective successors and assigns, heirs, administrators, and
representatives, and shall not be enforceable by, or inure to the benefit of, any other third party.

Section 4.02 Choice of Law. This Agreement shall be construed in accordance with, and
governed by, the internal law of the State of Rhode Island (without reference to its rules as to conflicts
of law).

Section 4.03 Modification. This Agreement may only be modified by a writing signed by all
of the parties hereto.

Section 4.04 Headings. The section headings herein are for convenience only and shall not
affect the construction thereof. Unless otherwise indicated, references to Sections and Articles are to
Sections and Articles, respectively, contained herein.

Section 4.05 Counterparts. This Agreement may be executed in one or more counterparts
but all such separate counterparts shall constitute but one and the same instrument; provided that,
although executed in counterparts, the executed signature pages of each such counterpart may be

40

===== PAGE 89 =====
[OCR]
affixed to a single copy of this Agreement which shall constitute an original. Counterparts delivered
by electronic transmission shall be deemed to be originally signed counterparts.

Section 4.06 Expenses and Attorney’s Fees. If any action is brought by any party to enforce
any provision of this Agreement, the prevailing party (as determined by final judgment of a court of
competent jurisdiction) will be entitled to recover its court costs, arbitration expenses and reasonable
attorneys’ fees.

PROPERTY DESCRIPTION (the “Property”): See Exhibit A attached hereto

The undersigned, on behalf of Seller, declares that, to the undersigned’s actual and current

knowledge, the following information is true and correct (as used herein, the phrase “the
undersigned’s actual and current knowledge” means the actual knowledge of the undersigned, after

1.

Purpose of Declaration. This declaration is made to the Title Company as an inducement
to it to complete a transaction concerning the Property. Seller acknowledges that Title
Company is relying upon the representations in this declaration as being true and correct
and that the transaction contemplated would not be consummated without this declaration
being executed.
Debts and Liens. Except as indicated below, there are no loans, tax liens, abstract of
judgment liens or other real estate liens affecting the Property that are not of public record.
Exceptions:
Real Property and Personal Property Taxes. All real property taxes assessed against the
Property which are due and payable have been paid in full, except as shown on the public
records.
Improvement debts and liens. During the six (6) months immediately preceding the date of
this declaration, Seller has not contracted with any person to whom a debt is due for labor
or materials furnished in the erection, alteration, repair or removal of a building or structure

furnishing such labor or materials for work performed during the past six (6) months.

, 2021, by and between Seller and Purchaser, there are no other unreleased

Schedule 19(c

Brentwood Nursing Home currently operates under a Time-Limited Federal Life Safety Code
Waiver (the “LSC Waiver”) pursuant to a Waiver Approval Letter dated June 19, 2017 from the
Centers for Medicare & Medicaid Services (“CMS”). The LSC Waiver will! expire on March 15,
2022.

As of December 16, 2016 (the “New Compliance Date”), CMS updated the means by which
Long Term Care Facilities (“LTCFs”) may demonstrate compliance with the fire safety
requirements of the 2012 Life Safety Code and the 2012 Health Care Facilities Code (the “Fire
Safety Requirements”). Prior to the New Compliance Date, LTCFs could demonstrate
compliance with the Fire Safety Requirements by achieving a passing score on the 2001 edition
of the National Fire Protection Association’s Fire Safety Evaluation System (the “2001 FSES”).
Following the New Compliance Date, the 2001 FSES was phased out as a tool for demonstrating
compliance with the Fire Safety Requirements, and it was replaced by the 2013 edition of the
National Fire Protection Association’s Fire Safety Evaluation System (the “2013 FSES”).

Following the New Compliance Date, Brentwood Nursing Home did not achieve a passing score
on the 2013 FSES. As a result, Brentwood Nursing Home obtained the LSC Waiver and engaged
a consultant to recommend steps for achieving a passing score on the 2013 FSES so as to
demonstrate compliance with the Fire Safety Requirements. Brentwood Nursing Home has
followed the recommendations of the consultant, and the consultant has submitted a letter to
CMS indicting that Brentwood Nursing Home is presently in compliance with the Fire Safety
Requirements. It is expected that CMS will promptly issue a letter that Brentwood Nursing
Home is in compliance with the Fire Safety Requirements.

AMENDMENT TO ASSET PURCHASE AGREEMENT

This AMENDMENT TO ASSET PURCHASE AGREEMENT (this “Amendment’) is
made as of the 3% day of November, 2021, by and among the following entities, as
sellers: (i) 4000 POST ROAD LLC, a Rhode Island limited liability company, (ii) 4040
POST ROAD LLC, a Rhode Island limited liability company, (iii) P & R CORONA
REALTY, LLC, a Rhode Island limited liability company, (iv) AGIM, LLC, a Rhode
Island limited liability company (collectively, (i) through (iv), “Seller’); and the following
entities, as purchasers: (v) 83 CORONA STREET EDEN REALTY LLC, a Rhode Island
limited liability company, (vi) 4040 POST ROAD EDEN REALTY LLC, a Rhode Island
limited liability company, (vii) 73 CORONA STREET EDEN REALTY LLC, a Rhode
Island limited liability company, (viii) 62 FREY STREET EDEN REALTY LLC, a Rhode
Island limited liability company (collectively, (v) through (viii), “Purchaser’).

Recitals

On or about August 4, 2021, Seller and Purchaser entered into that certain Asset
Purchase Agreement (the “Asset Agreement’), pursuant to which Seller agreed to sell,
and Purchaser agreed to purchase, the Purchased Assets (as defined in the Asset
Agreement). Contemporaneously therewith, certain affiliates of Seller (the “Current
Operators’) entered into an Operations Transfer Agreement (the “OTA") with certain
affiliates of Purchaser (the “New Operators’), pursuant to which the Current Operators
agreed to make an orderly transfer of operations at the Facilities (as defined in the
OTA), and the New Operators agreed to accept such transfer. Contemporaneously
herewith, the Current Operators and the New Operators are entering into an
amendment to the OTA (the “OTA Amendment’). Pursuant to Section 14(a) of the
Asset Agreement, the Closing Date under the Asset Agreement was designated as the
date which is the first calendar month following thirty (30) days from the date on which
the New Operators obtain the Required Authorizations (as defined in the Asset
Agreement). Notwithstanding that certain of the conditions precedent set forth in
Sections 10, 11 and 14 of the Asset Agreement (including, without limitation,
Purchaser's and/or New Operators’ receipt of the Required Authorizations) have not
been fulfilled, Seller and Purchaser desire to close on the purchase and sale of the
Purchased Assets upon and subject to the conditions set forth herein.

Seller and Purchaser, therefore, mutually agree as follows:

Agreements

1. Terms. Capitalized terms used in this Amendment and not otherwise defined
herein shall have the meanings ascribed to them in the Asset Agreement.

2. Closing Date. Seller and Purchaser each hereby waive any provision contained
in the Asset Agreement which conditions the Closing under the Asset Agreement upon
the Purchaser's and/or the New Operators’ receipt of any form of licensing approval
from the Rhode Island Department of Health, including, without limitation, the Required

===== PAGE 109 =====
[OCR]
Authorizations (as defined in the Asset Agreement) and the License Approval (as
defined in the OTA). Accordingly, Seller and Purchaser hereby agree that the Closing
Date for the Purchased Assets shall be on or before December 31, 2021. Seller and
Purchaser further agree that the closing under the OTA (the “OTA Closing’) shal! occur
on a future date following the Closing Date under the Asset Agreement (as amended by
this Amendment) and not contemporaneously with the Closing Date under the Asset
Agreement, and in accordance with the terms of the OTA Amendment.

3. Purchase Price and Escrow. The Purchase Price shall be paid by the
Purchaser to the Seller in full on the Closing Date, in accordance with Section 2 of the
Asset Agreement and subject to an additional escrow amount set forth as follows. At the
Closing, a portion of the Purchase Price in the amount of One Million Nine Hundred
Thirty-Five Thousand Four Hundred and Fifty ($1,935,450) Dollars (the “Operations
Escrow’) shall be deposited with the Escrow Agent. The Operations Escrow shall be
teleased to Seller simultaneously with the consummation of the OTA Closing.

4. Allocations. Purchaser shall not disclose to the Rhode Island Department of
Health or any other person, orally, in writing, or electronically, at any time, the total
Purchase Price or the amount of any portion(s) of the Purchase Price allocated
specifically to the Brentwood Bay Property, or the operations of the assisted living
facility situated thereon, or any aggregate amounts from which such allocations are
capable of being calculated. For purposes of submission to the Rhode Island
Department of Health: (i) the amount of the Purchase Price in Section 2 of the Asset
Agreement shall be redacted, and (ii) Schedule C to the Asset Agreement shall be
redacted in the manner of the attached Schedule to this Amendment to Asset
Agreement.

5. Ratification. Except as herein modified, the Asset Agreement is hereby ratified,
confirmed, and approved in all respects.

OPERATIONS TRANSFER AGREEMENT.

THIS OPERATIONS TRANSFER AGREEMENT (this “Agreement”) is entered into
as of August 4th , 2021 (the “Execution Date”), by and between each of the Current Operator
entities listed and designated on the signature page below (each a “Current Operator” and,
collectively, the “Current Operators”), and each of the New Operators entities listed and
designated on the signature page below, each a Rhode Island limited liability company (each a
“New Operator” and, collectively, the “New Operators”).

RECITALS

WHEREAS, Current Operators operate those certain skilled nursing facilities and that
certain assisted living facility as more particularly described on Exhibit A attached hereto and
made a part hereof (each a “Facility” and collectively the “Facilities”), each under a lease (the

“Existing Lease”) with an affiliate of the Current Operators (the “Seller”) and as described on
Exhibit A.

WHEREAS, contemporaneously herewith, Seller or its assigns, as owner of the Facilities
and the real property comprising each Facility, are entering into that certain Asset Purchase
Agreement (the “APA”) of even date herewith, pursuant to which, subject to the terms and
conditions contained therein, Seller will sell to an affiliate of New Operators or its assigns
(“Purchaser”), and Purchaser will purchase from Seller, the Real Property (as defined in the APA)
and improvements relating to the Facilities as more particularly described therein;

WHEREAS, in conjunction with the execution of the APA, Current Operators and New
Operators desire to enter into this Agreement to facilitate an orderly transition of each Facility’s
operations from Current Operators to New Operators.

NOW, THEREFORE, for the mutual promises, representations, warranties and covenants
contained herein and in the APA, and other good and valuable consideration, the receipt and
sufficiency of which are hereby mutually acknowledged by the parties hereto, intending to be
legally bound, the parties agree as follows:

ils Commencement Date. This Agreement shall commence and be effective on and as of the
Closing Date as defined in the APA (the “Commencement Date”) provided all conditions
precedent set forth in this Agreement have been fully satisfied or have been waived in writing.
Current Operators agree to cooperate with New Operators, and New Operators agree to cooperate
with Current Operators, to affect an orderly transfer of the operations of the Facilities as of the
Commencement Date. Such cooperation requires prompt delivery of required information related
to each current Facility’s operations, including, without limitation, employee demographic
information related to health insurance. For clarity, cooperation includes providing a reasonably
substantive response to phone calls and emails not less than three (3) days after delivery or if the
communication is delivered after 10 AM local time for the recipient on a Friday, then by noon on
the next business day.

===== PAGE 117 =====
[OCR]
2. Conditions Precedent.

a. New Operators’ obligation to consummate the transactions contemplated in this
Agreement shall be subject to the following conditions precedent on and as of the Commencement
Date to the reasonable satisfaction of New Operators or the waiver thereof by New Operators,

which waiver shall be binding upon New Operators only to the extent made in writing and dated
as of the Commencement Date:

i. Current Operators shall have duly and timely performed and fulfilled all of
its duties, obligations, promises, covenants and agreements hereunder.
ii. Delivery by Current Operators of a fully executed Bill of Sale to convey to

New Operators title in and to all assets transferred by Current Operators to New Operators under
this Agreement (the “Transferred Assets”) in substantially the form annexed hereto as Exhibit B.

iii. Delivery by Current Operators of a fully executed General Assignment of
the Intangible Property for the Facilities in substantially the form annexed hereto as Exhibit C.
iv. Delivery by Current Operators of a duly executed counterpart signature
page to the Escrow Holdback Agreement (as defined in the APA).
v. Seller and Current Operators shall have terminated the Existing Lease.
vi. Current Operators shall not be in material breach of any term, provision, or

condition of this Agreement. The representations and warranties given by Current Operators to
New Operators hereunder shall be true and correct, as of such date.

vii. Consummation and closing of the transactions contemplated under the APA
and accordingly all of the conditions to closing set forth in the APA are hereby incorporated herein
by reference, it being understood and agreed that, other than those rights granted to and obligations
imposed on Current Operators and/or New Operators which are specifically required to be
performed prior to Commencement Date, neither Current Operators nor New Operators shall have
any rights or obligations hereunder if the closing of the transaction set forth in the APA fails to
occur.

viii. New Operators shall have obtained all government approvals that are
required to operate each Facility under Rhode Island law, including, without limitation, the skilled
nursing facility licenses and the assisted living facility license (collectively, the “Licenses”) or a
comfort letter from the Rhode Island Department of Health (“RIDOH”), indicating its intent to
issue the Licenses subject only to the closing of the transaction contemplated in the APA.

ix, No action or proceeding shall have been instituted, nor any judgment, order
or decree entered by any court or governmental body or authority preventing the consummation of
the transaction contemplated by this Agreement or the APA, or which could materially and
adversely affect New Operators’ ability to operate each Facility as a nursing home or an assisted
living facility with the same number and type of beds as are operating at the Facility on the date
hereof.

x. On the Commencement Date, Current Operators shall provide New
Operators with evidence that it has procured a tail insurance policy with respect to Current
Operators’ professional liability insurance policy providing coverage for claims made in the three-
year period following the Commencement Date and with coverage limits not less than the coverage
in effect until such policy immediately prior to the Commencement Date.

===== PAGE 118 =====
[OCR]
xi. No material adverse change shall have occurred in the census, physical
condition or business of the Facilities, Current Operators, or with respect to the Supplies (as
hereinafter defined), whether financial or otherwise. Without limiting the generality of the
foregoing, it is acknowledged that the following shall constitute a material adverse change:

1, A total resident census of less than forty-two (42) residents for the
Sunny View Nursing Home Facility;

2. A total resident census of less than seventy-two (72) residents for
the Brentwood Health Center Facility; and

3. A total resident census of less than fifty-four (54) residents for the
Brentwood by the Bay Facility.

xii. Except as disclosed pursuant to Section 18(h) and/or Section 18(x) hereof,
none of the Facilities are in an open survey cycle (i.e., received violations but not yet found to be
in substantial compliance). In the event that any survey or action by any governmental or quasi-
governmental organization issues a survey violation equivalent to an F-Tag or greater, or any
action that results in a reduction in the United States Department of Health and Human Services,
Centers for Medicare and Medicaid Services (“CMS”) star rating of any Facility, then such event
shall be deemed to be a material adverse change.

b. Current Operators’ obligation to consummate the transactions contemplated in this
Agreement shall be subject to the following conditions precedent on and as of the Commencement
Date to the reasonable satisfaction of Current Operators or the waiver thereof by Current
Operators, which waiver shall be binding upon Current Operators only to the extent made in
writing and dated as of the Commencement Date:

i. New Operators shall have duly and timely performed and fulfilled all of its
duties, obligations, promises, covenants and agreements hereunder.
ii. New Operators shall not be in material breach of any term, provision or

condition of this Agreement. The representations and warranties given by New Operators to
Current Operators hereunder shall be true and correct, as of such date.

iii. Delivery by New Operators of a duly executed counterpart signature page
to the Escrow Holdback Agreement.

or decree entered by any court or governmental body or authority preventing the consummation of

adversely affect New Operators’ ability to operate each Facility as a nursing home or an assisted
living facility with the same number and type of beds as are operating at the Facility on the date
hereof.

v. Consummation and closing of the transactions contemplated under the APA
and accordingly all of the conditions to closing set forth in the APA are hereby incorporated herein
by reference, it being understood and agreed that, other than those rights granted to and obligations
imposed on Current Operators and/or New Operators which are specifically required to be

to occur.

5. Transfer of Resident Trust Funds and Property.

a. On or prior to the Commencement Date, Current Operators shall provide to New
Operators a true, correct, and complete accounting (properly reconciled), certified as being true,
correct, and complete by Current Operators, of any resident trust funds and an inventory of all
residents’ property, if any, held by Current Operators for residents at each Facility (collectively,
the “Resident Trust Funds”).

b. Effective as of the Commencement Date, Current Operators shall transfer all of
Current Operators’ right, title and interest in the Resident Trust Funds to New Operators and New
Operators shall assume all of Current Operators’ right, title and interest in the Resident Trust Funds
in trust for the residents, in accordance with applicable statutory and regulatory requirements.
Current Operators shall comply with all governmental statutes, rules, and regulations with respect
to the transfer of such Resident Trust Funds. New Operators hereby agree that it will accept the
Resident Trust Funds in trust for the residents, in accordance with applicable statutory and
regulatory requirements, provided, however such transfer shall not relieve Current Operators of its
custodial and fiduciary responsibilities for such funds and property to the beneficiaries thereof for
the period prior to the Commencement Date. Within two (2) business days after the
Commencement Date, Current Operators and New Operators will prepare a final schedule of the
Resident Trust Funds and reconcile and pay over the Resident Trust Funds to New Operators, in
trust for the residents, and New Operators shall thereafter hold and administer such funds in
accordance with applicable law.

c. Current Operators will allow New Operators to use Current Operators’ Resident
Fund Management Service (“RFMS”) accounts, and New Operators will pay all fees and costs
associated with using Current Operators’ RFMS accounts, until all RFMS accounts are transferred
to New Operators.

d. Current Operators will indemnify, defend, and hold New Operators harmless from
all liabilities, claims, demands and causes of action of any nature whatsoever, including reasonable
attorneys’ fees, in the event the amount of funds, if any, transferred to New Operators did not
tepresent the full amount of the funds shown to have been delivered to Current Operators as
custodian or with respect to any Resident Trust Funds delivered, or claimed to have been delivered,
to Current Operators, but which were not delivered by Current Operators to New Operators, for

diligently pursuing a CMS Form 855a on or before the Commencement Date, noting that the New
Operators’ license to operate the Facilities has been applied for and will be provided at a later date.

and all rights and liabilities relating to the existing Medicare Provider Numbers for any and all

Operators’ obligations hereunder nor release Current Operators from any such duty or obligation.

managers, (ii) liabilities or obligations of the Current Operators with respect to any Old Contracts,

obligations of the Current Operators for any federal, state, county, or local taxes applicable to or

Operators, (vi) other liabilities resulting from any act or failure to act by Current Operators prior
to the Commencement Date, (vii) any liabilities related to any Current Operators’ pension fund or
pension fund equivalent; (viii) any of Current Operators’ obligations related to COVID-19
Paycheck Protection Program loans (“PPP”) or other government stimulus or relief program, or
any non-compliance with the terms of a PPP loan or other government stimulus or relief program
(including any future determination that a borrower was ineligible to participate in such program),
or the inability to secure PPP loan forgiveness due to improper use of proceeds pre-closing or
otherwise; (ix) any life care contract, entrance fee, founders’ fee, or any similar contract or fee
which may become due after the Transfer Date with any resident of the Facility; or (x) any goods
or services that were ordered and consumed prior to the Commencement Date by Current Operator
but invoiced after the Commencement Date.

c. Current Operators do not assume and shall not be liable for any other debts,
liabilities, or obligations of the New Operators including without limitation (i) liabilities or
obligations of New Operators to its creditors, members, or managers, (ii) liabilities or obligations
of New Operators with respect to any contracts, acts, events, or transactions occurring on or after
the Commencement Date, (iii) liabilities or obligations of New Operators for any federal, state,
county, or local taxes applicable to or assessed against New Operators or the assets or business of
New Operators, or applicable to, incurred by and accrued or assessed against the Facilities after
the Commencement Date, (iv) any contingent liabilities or obligations of New Operators, whether
known or unknown by New Operators or Current Operators, or (v) any other liabilities resulting
from any act or failure to act by New Operators on or after the Commencement Date.

d. Except as specifically provided in this Agreement, New Operators shall have no
duty whatsoever to take any action or receive or make any payment or credit arising from or related
to any services provided or costs incurred in connection with the management and operation of the
Facilities prior to the Commencement Date, including any matters relating to contracts, cost

9. Accounts Receivable, Refunds, Credits; Accounts Payable.

a. Current Operators shall retain the right to collect all unpaid accounts receivable,
refunds, and credits related to the period prior to the Commencement Date with respect to the
Facilities, but only to the extent that such accounts receivable relate to services rendered prior to
the Commencement Date.

b. Payments for Designated Dates of Service. If New Operators receive payment on
any accounts receivable, refunds or other obligations owing from any institutional third-party
payor or from any person on behalf of a private-pay resident, and if such payment is designated,
in whole or in part, or accompanied by an invoice, as to specific dates of service provided by
Current Operators, New Operators shall promptly remit such amounts (in whole or in part as to
such dates) to Current Operators. New Operators shall otherwise have no obligation for collection
of Current Operators’ accounts receivable, but shall provide reasonable cooperation and assistance
to Current Operators in Current Operators’ efforts to collect such amounts. If Current Operators
receive payment on any accounts receivable, refunds, or other obligations owing from any
institutional third-party payor or from any person on behalf of a private-pay resident, and if such
payment is designated, in whole or in part, as to specific dates of service provided by New
Operators, Current Operators shall promptly remit such amounts (in whole or in part as to such
dates) to New Operators. Current Operator shall otherwise have no obligation for collection of
New Operators’ accounts receivable, but shall provide reasonable cooperation and assistance to
New Operators in New Operators’ efforts to collect such amounts.

c Payments without Designation as to Dates of Service. Five (5) business days prior
to the Commencement Date, Current Operators shall provide New Operators with a complete
aging of all private pay residents. Current Operators and New Operators shall review each private
pay resident in arrears and shall mutually agree on a case-by-case basis how to allocate post-
closing payments which are not designated as to time period covered. Undesignated payments
made from or on behalf of private pay residents that are thirty (30) days or less in arrears, shall be
first applied to Current Operators’ current outstanding balance. Undesignated payments made from
or on behalf of private pay residents that are thirty (30) days or more in arrears, shall be first applied
to New Operators’ current outstanding balance.

d. If at any time after the Commencement Date, Current Operators or New Operators
shall receive any payment from any federal or state agency for services rendered at the Facilities,
and the payment is not accompanied by a statement specifying the dates of service to which it
applies and such payment is received within forty-five (45) days of the Commencement Date, then
payment shall first be applied to the current outstanding accounts receivable liabilities of Current
Operators first, with any remainder applied to any previously outstanding account receivables of
New Operators.

e. To the extent either party receives any other payments for accounts receivable of
the other party, both parties acknowledge that the party receiving the payment belonging to the
other party shall hold the payment in trust, that neither party shall have any right to offset with
respect to such accounts receivable, and that the party erroneously receiving the payment shall
have no right, title or interest whatsoever in the payment and shall remit the same to the other
within ten (10) business days after such receipt.

f. All accounts payable for services provided or goods furnished for or at the Facilities
prior to the Commencement Date shall remain the sole responsibility and obligation of Current

12

===== PAGE 127 =====
[OCR]
Operators. All accounts payable for services provided or goods furnished for or at the Facilities on
or after the Commencement Date shall be the sole responsibility and obligation of New Operators.
To the extent accounts payable have been accrued for a period that includes time both before and
after the Commencement Date, the parties hereto shall apportion the responsibility for payment of
the same on a pro rata basis. The parties hereto hereby agree to cooperate with each other and
notify the merchants, suppliers, or other third parties with respect to which of Current Operators
or New Operators bears responsibility for accounts payable of the Facilities based on the foregoing
clauses of this Section. For clarity, responsibility for payments for goods or services delivered on
or after the Commencement Date will be New Operators’.

10. Employees.

a. At a time mutually agreed to by Current Operators and New Operators, Current
Operators and New Operators shall jointly notify all employees providing services at the Facilities
(the “Current Employees”) of the transactions contemplated in this Agreement (the “Employee
Notification”). Prior to the Employee Notification, New Operators shall not meet or talk to any
Current Employees except as specifically agreed to in writing by Current Operators in its sole
discretion. Following the Employee Notification, New Operators shall be entitled to meet and talk
to the Current Employees at times reasonably agreed upon by the parties to avoid disrupting the
Facilities, its residents, and the Current Employees.

b. Current Operators shall terminate the employment of all the Current Employees as
of the Commencement Date. Upon such termination, New Operators shall not be bound by or
assume any employment contracts to which Current Operators may be a party. Current Operators
shall not make any material changes in the compensation or benefits of the employees at the
Facility prior to the Commencement Date.

ce: New Operators shall have no obligation and shall suffer no liability if it does not
choose to rehire or offer to rehire some or all of the tenmninated Current Employees.
Notwithstanding the foregoing, except as disclosed to Current Operators prior to the
Commencement Date, New Operators shall rehire or offer to rehire such terminated Current
Employees at wages and benefits sufficient to avoid the applicability of the Workers Adjustment
and Retraining Notification Act, 29 U.S.C. § 2101 and any similar state and local laws and
regulations, pursuant to employment terms acceptable to New Operators (hereinafter, the
“Retained Employees”). For the avoidance of doubt, Current Operators cannot guarantee or offer
New Operators any commitment that any or all of the Current Employees who are terminated in
accordance with Section 10(b) shall agree to be retained by New Operators. Nothing in this
paragraph shall create any right in favor of any person not a party hereto, including without
limitation, the Current Employees, or constitute an employment agreement or condition of
employment for any employee of Current Operators or any affiliate of Current Operators who is a
Retained Employee.

d. New Operators and Current Operators agree and acknowledge that the employees
at the Facilities provide valuable services that are crucial for the success of the Facilities, and New
Operators’ decision to serve as licensed operator of the Facilities is based upon the skills and
qualifications of such employees. As such, no person or entity that either directly or indirectly
controls, is under common control with or is otherwise affiliated with Current Operators shall,
during the period beginning on the Commencement Date and ending upon the date that is two (2)
years after the Commencement Date, solicit, directly or indirectly, any Retained Employee of New

13

===== PAGE 128 =====
[OCR]
Operators. The parties also agree that (i) advertisements available to the general public, such as
through website job postings and newspaper, Internet and trade journals shall not constitute
solicitation for purposes of this Section and (ii) nothing shall prohibit hiring if a Retained
Employee’s employment with New Operators is terminated.

e. Accrued Compensation and Benefits. Current Operators shall remain solely
responsible for payment of all Gncluding any accrued or owing) salaries, wages, bonuses, expense
reimbursement, sick, personal and vacation pay and paid time off benefits, retirement plan
contributions, pension plans, deferred compensation, and any other employee benefits, including
severance pay, if any, earned by employees of the Facilities through the Commencement Date,
together with any and all accrued payroll taxes, workers compensation premiums, and any other
costs, claims, demands, or liabilities in connection with employees of the Facilities, whether or not
hired by New Operators, through the Commencement Date. Current Operators shall indemnify,
defend, and hold New Operators harmless from all such costs and liabilities. Current Operators
shall provide a list of all employee information for those employees hired by New Operators to
New Operators at least seven (7) days prior to the Commencement Date and shall be updated
concurrent with the Commencement Date and Current Operators shall represent and warrant to
New Operators that such information is true, correct and complete (which shall survive the
Commencement Date).

Operators, at no expense to Current Operators, with the goal that the Retained Employees will not

13.‘ Prorations.

a. Revenues and expenses pertaining to the Facilities, utility charges for the billing
period in which the Commencement Date occurs, Assumed Contracts, employee accruals, prepaid
income and expenses, Rhode Island bed taxes, and other related items of revenue or expense
attributable to the Facilities shall be prorated between Current Operators and New Operators as of
the Commencement Date.

b. All such prorations shall be made on the basis of actual days elapsed in the relevant
accounting or revenue period and shall be based on the most recent information available to the
parties hereto. Utility charges which are not metered and read on the Commencement Date shall
be estimated based on prior charges, and shall be re-prorated upon receipt of statements therefor.
Current Operators and New Operators shall jointly arrange for the turnover of the utility services
and under no circumstances shall Current Operators arrange for shut off of any utilities. For the
avoidance of doubt, Current Operators shall be solely responsible for the prompt payment for any
goods or services ordered and consumed by Current Operators for its account but invoiced after
the Commencement Date.

c. Except as otherwise set forth herein, all amounts owing from one party hereto to
the other party hereto that require adjustment after the Commencement Date shall be settled within
thirty (30) days after the Commencement Date or, in the event the information necessary for such
adjustment is not available within said thirty (30) day period, as soon thereafter as reasonably
practicable.

15. Obligations of the Parties Prior to Closing

a. Federal and State Regulatory Approvals. Within fifteen (15) days following the
Execution Date, New Operators shall file all applications with the appropriate State agency or
department in order to obtain approval from the RIDOH for the change of ownership of the
Facilities to New Operators and issuance of a new operating license to New Operators (the “New
Operators License”) effective (retroactively, if applicable) as of the Commencement Date (the
“License Approval’). New Operators shall diligently pursue the issuance of the License Approval
and Current Operators shall cooperate with New Operators in connection with the obtaining of the
License Approval. As soon as practicable after receipt of the License Approval, but in any event
prior to the Commencement Date, Current Operators shall sign and New Operators shall file and
diligently pursue CMS Form 855a or any other necessary form for any of the Facilities with the
appropriate third parties within the appropriate notification periods. Current Operators shall
provide such information and cooperate with New Operators as necessary in the preparation of the
Form 855a or any other necessary form and all other provider agreement applications.

b. Condition of Facilities. Between the Execution Date and the Commencement Date,
except as otherwise permitted under this Agreement, Current Operators shall: (a) maintain each
Facility in substantially the same condition as the particular Facility is in as of the Execution Date,
ordinary wear and tear excepted, (b) operate each Facility in material compliance with all
applicable laws, rules and regulations and in the ordinary course and in substantially the same
manner as the particular Facility was operated before the Execution Date, including all marketing,
advertising and other efforts to attract and retain patients and residents, (c) maintain not less than
a five (5) day or otherwise greater minimum amount of supplies as may be required under
applicable law and as shall be necessary for the proper operation of each Facility, (d) take no
actions to close any Facility, change the number of licensed beds, or change the Medicare or
Medicaid certification status of any Facility, (e) promptly notify New Operators upon becoming
aware of any third party claims or action that will give rise to any filings with any regulatory or
government agencies that materially adversely affect Current Operators or the Facilities, (f)
maintain in force the existing hazard and liability insurance policies, or comparable coverage, for
the Facilities as now in effect, (g) not enter into any new leases or contracts or change any lease or
contract, except residency agreements in the usual course of business, or perform any action that
materially adversely affect the value of the Transferred Assets or the Facilities, (h) maintain all of
its books and records in accordance with past practice, (i) keep in full force all licenses and Permits,
(j) satisfy and discharge all claims, liens, security interests, tenancies, liabilities or other financial
obligations which constitute a lien or encumbrance on any of the Transferred Assets, (k) file all
returns, reports and filings of any kind or nature, including but not limited to, cost reports, required
to be filed by Current Operators on a timely basis, and (1) pay when due (or withhold and pay over,
as required) all taxes, assessments, charges, and levies imposed upon Current Operators, the
Transferred Assets, or the Facilities (or any portion thereof), but shall have the right to challenge
any such taxes, assessments, charges and levies following payment thereof and procedures
established by the taxing authority.

16. Indemnification.

a. Survival. The representations, warranties, and indemnifications contained herein,
and in any agreements, certificates or other instruments delivered pursuant hereto, shall survive
the Commencement Date and the consummation of the transactions contemplated hereby, for a
period of two (2) years, regardless of any investigation made by, or on behalf of, any party hereto,
but subject to all limitations and other provisions contained in this Agreement.

db. In addition to any other indemnification provided in this Agreement, New
Operators agree to protect, indemnify, save, defend and hold harmless Current Operators and its
successors, assigns, affiliates, managers, members, agents, servants and employees from and
against any and all costs, expenses, claims, demands, obligations, losses, liabilities, damages,
recoveries and deficiencies (including interest penalties and reasonable attorneys’ fees, costs and
expenses) (collectively, “Losses”) suffered by Current Operators by reason of or resulting from
(a) any material breach or material inaccuracy of any clause, representation, warranty, obligation
or covenant of New Operators contained in or made pursuant to this Agreement (including any
schedule or exhibit hereto) or the APA; (b) New Operators’ ownership of the Transferred Assets
and Personal Property on and after the Commencement Date; and (c) New Operators’ operation of
the Facilities from and after the Commencement Date.

c. In addition to any other indemnification provided in this Agreement, Current
Operators agrees to protect, indemnify, save, defend and hold harmless New Operators and its
successors, assigns, affiliates, managers, members, agents, servants and employees from and
against any and all Losses suffered by New Operators by reason of or resulting from (a) any
material breach or material inaccuracy of any clause, representation, or warranty of Current

===== PAGE 132 =====
[OCR]
Operators contained in or made pursuant to this Agreement or the APA; (b) Current Operators’
ownership of the Transferred Assets and Personal Property prior to the Commencement Date; (c)
any Excluded Property; (d) the operation of the Facilities prior to the Commencement Date,
including but not limited to any and all third party claims, employee claims or federal or state
government claims related to the licensure and certification of the Facilities, any employee wage
claim, or any employee pensions claims, the facts giving rise to which occurred prior to the
Commencement Date; and (d) any of Current Operators’ obligations related to a PPP loan or other
government stimulus or relief program, or any non-compliance with the terms of a PPP loan or
other government stimulus or relief program (including any future determination that a borrower
was ineligible to participate in such program), or the inability to secure PPP loan forgiveness due
to improper use of proceeds pre-closing or otherwise.

d. In the event that any liability, claim, demand or cause of action which is
indemnified against by or under any term, provision, section, or paragraph of this Agreement
(“Indemnitee’s Claim”) is made against or received by any indemnified party (“Indemnitee”)
hereunder, said Indemnitee shall notify the indemnifying party (“Indemnitor’) in writing within
seven (7) calendar days of Indemnitee’s receipt of written notice of said Indemnitee’s Claim,
provided, however, that Indemnitee’s failure to timely notify Indemnitor of Indemnitee’s receipt
of an Indemnitee’s Claim shall not impair, void, vitiate or invalidate Indemnitor’s indemnity
hereunder nor release Indemnitor from the same, which duty, obligation and indemnity shall
remain valid, binding, enforceable and in full force and effect so long as Indemnitee’s delay in
notifying Indemnitor does not, solely by itself, directly and materially prejudice Indemmnitor’s right
or ability to defend the Indemnified Claim. Upon its receipt of any or all Indemnitee’s Claim(s),
Indemnitor shall diligently defend, compromise or settle said Indemnitee’s Claim at Indemnitor’s
sole and exclusive cost and expense and shall promptly provide Indemnitee evidence thereof
within fourteen (14) calendar days of the final, unappealable resolution of said Indemnitee’s Claim.
Upon the receipt of the written request of Indemnitee, Indemnitor shall within seven (7) calendar
days provide Indemnitee a true, correct, accurate and complete written status report regarding the
then current status of said Indemnitee’s Claim. Under no circumstances shall either party be
required to indemnify the other party for such other party’s own negligence. Notwithstanding the
foregoing, Indemnitor’s duty to defend shall not be abridged or in any way relieved based upon
allegations that Indemnitee was contributorily negligent for any claim brought hereunder.
Indemnitor’s duty to defend Indemnitee shall commence immediately upon the filing of any claim,
action, lawsuit, hearing, demand, or arbitration related to any indemnifiable claim regardless of
whether Indemnitor may have any cross or third-party claims against Indemnitee or whether
Indemnitor is granted or denied insurance coverage by its insurance carrier(s). If it is later proven
that Indemnitee was the sole and exclusive cause, or willfully caused or contributed to the damages
being sought in such proceeding, Indemnitee shall reimburse Indemnitor for the proportionate
amount that such defense fees and damages were incurred by Indemnitor, but not for defense fees
or damages paid by Indemnitor’s insurance carrier(s).

e. In the event that Indemnitor fails or refuses to indemnify, save, defend, protect or
hold Indemnitee harmless from and against an Indemnitee’s Claim or to diligently pursue the same
to its conclusion, or in the event that Indemnitor fails to timely report to Indemnitee the status of
its efforts to reach a final resolution of an Indemnitee’s Claim on seven (7) calendar days prior

18

===== PAGE 133 =====
[OCR]
written notice to Indemnitor during which time Indemnitor may cure any alleged default
hereunder, the foregoing shall immediately, automatically and without further notice be an Event
of Default hereunder (an “Indemnification Default”) and thereafter Indemnitee may, but shall not
be obligated to, immediately and upon notice to Indemnitor, intervene in and defend, settle or
compromise said Indemnitee’s Claim at Indemnitor’s sole and exclusive cost and expense,
including but not limited to attorneys’ fees, and, thereafter, within seven (7) calendar days of
written demand for the same Indemnitor shall promptly reimburse Indemnitee all said
Indemnitee’s Claims and the reasonable costs, expenses and attorneys’ fees incurred by Indemnitee
to defend, settle or compromise said Indemnitee’s Claims plus interest thereon from the date
incurred until paid in full at the then current prime rate of interest (as announced from time to time
by the Wall Street Journal) plus three percent (3%).

f. Prior to an Indemnification Default, Indemnitee may not settle or compromise an
Indemnitee’s Claim without Indemnitor’s prior written consent, which shall not be unreasonably
withheld. Failure to obtain such consent shall be deemed a forfeiture by Indemnitee of its
indemnification rights hereunder.

g. Unless otherwise indicated herein, the indemnifications under this Section shall
survive the Commencement Date for a period of two (2) years. Further, with respect to any matter
as to which a claim has been asserted hereunder by timely delivery of a written notice of an
indemnification claim to the Indemnitor (an “Indemnification Claims Notice”) and is pending or
unresolved at the end of the foregoing period, such claim shall continue to be covered by the
indemnification provision hereof, and the Indemnitor shall remain liable therefor, until finally
terminated or otherwise resolved.

h. In the event that an Indemnitee could reasonably be entitled to recover any sum
(whether by payment, discount, credit or otherwise) from any third party (which shall include
insurers) in respect of any matter for which an indemnification claim could be made against an
Indemnitor, the Indemnitee shall first use commercially reasonable efforts to recover such sum
from such third party including appeals, provided such obligation shall not stay the period of time
in which an Indemnitee must provide notice of an Indemnitee’s Claim. Any sum actually recovered
by an Indemnitee (less any reasonable costs and expenses incurred by an Indemnitee in recovering
such sum) will reduce the amount of such claim. If an Indemnitor pays an Indemnitee an amount
in respect of, and an Indemnitee subsequently receives from a third party a sum which refers to
that payment, the Indemnitee shall forthwith repay to the Indemnitor so much of the amount paid
by the Indemnitor as does not exceed the sum recovered by the Indemnitee from the third party
less all reasonable costs, charges and expenses incurred by the Indemnitee in obtaining that
payment and in recovering that sum from the third party. An Indemnitor’s obligations under this
Section are stayed pending the outcome of the Indemnitee’s efforts to secure third party payment.
If, after an Indemnitor has paid in full any claim hereunder in respect of a tax liability, and
Indemnitee receives a payment in respect of such tax liability, the Indemnitee shall repay to the
Indemnitor a sum corresponding to the amount of such refund after deduction from such repayment
of an amount equal to any reasonable costs incurred by the Indemnitee in obtaining it.

i, Mitigation. The Indemnitee agrees that: (i) it shall act reasonably and in good faith
in an effort to mitigate any losses to which it is entitled to indemnification; and (ii) that the
Indemnitor shall be entitled to meaningfully participate in such mitigation by the Indemnitee.

concern basis) of such person’s assets and property will, as of such date, exceed the amounts
required to pay its debts as they become absolute and mature, as of such date, (b) such person will
have adequate capital to carry on its business and (c) such person will be able to pay its debts as
they become absolute and mature, in the ordinary course of business, taking into account the timing
of and amounts of cash to be received by it and the timing of and amounts of cash to be payable
on or in respect of its indebtedness.

20. Events of Default; Remedies. The breach by either party (“Defaulting Party”) hereto of
any term, provision, condition, promise, covenant, agreement, representation, warranty, guaranty,
indemnity, duty or obligation if not cured within ten (10) business days of the earlier of said
Defaulting Party’s receipt or refusal of written notice of the same from the other party (“Non-
Defaulting Party”) hereto shall automatically and without further notice hereunder be an
immediate event of default (“Event of Default”) entitling the Non-Defaulting Party to exercise
any and all remedies available to it hereunder or in law or equity, provided, however, that if a
non-monetary breach is not reasonably capable of being cured within the aforesaid ten (10)
business days but the Defaulting Party promptly commences to cure within said period, within said
period notifies the Non-Defaulting Party in writing of the commencement of said cure, and
thereafter diligently pursues the same to conclusion and successfully completes said cure within
thirty (30) calendar days of its first receipt of notice of said breach or violation, it shall not be an
Event of Default hereunder. The Non-Defaulting Party’s rights and remedies hereunder shall be
cumulative and not mutually exclusive and the exercise by the Non-Defaulting Party of one or

of this Agreement, this Agreement (i) shall terminate, without further notice or action by either

prior to the Commencement Date by:

true and correct in all material respects on and as of the Commencement Date;
b. Current Operators, if New Operators fail to perform and comply in all material

respects with all agreements, obligations and covenants contained in this Agreement that are
required to be performed or complied with by it at or prior to the Commencement Date; provided,
however, if any of such failure by New Operators is capable of being cured, then New Operators
shall have ten (10) calendar days after written notice from Current Operators to New Operators
specifying, in reasonable detail, such failure to cure the breach. If New Operators cures the breach
within such ten (10) calendar day period, then Current Operators shall not be entitled to terminate
this Agreement pursuant to this subsection.

c. New Operators or Purchaser at any time on or before the Due Diligence Expiration
Date pursuant to the APA;

d. New Operators, if the representations and warranties of Current Operators are not
true and correct in all material respects on and as of the Commencement Date;

e. New Operators, if Current Operators fails to perform and comply in all material
respects with all agreements, obligations and covenants contained in this Agreement that are
required to be performed or complied with by it at or prior to the Commencement Date; provided,
however, if any of such failure by Current Operators is capable of being cured, then Current
Operators shall have ten (10) calendar days after written notice from New Operators to Current
Operators specifying, in reasonable detail, such failure, to cure the breach. If Current Operators
cures the breach within such ten (10) calendar day period, then New Operators shall not be entitled
to terminate this Agreement pursuant to this subsection; and

f. New Operators, if any material condition precedent to New Operators’ obligations
hereunder have not been satisfied by the Commencement Date and New Operators has not waived
the conditions that were satisfied on or before the Commencement Date.

Schedule 18.h

Brentwood Nursing Home currently operates under a Time-Limited Federal Life Safety Code
Waiver (the “LSC Waiver’) pursuant to a Waiver Approval Letter dated June 19, 2017 from the

Centers for Medicare & Medicaid Services (“CMS”). The LSC Waiver will expire on March 15,
2022.

As of December 16, 2016 (the “New Compliance Date”), CMS updated the means by which Long
Term Care Facilities (“LTCFs”) may demonstrate compliance with the fire safety requirements of
the 2012 Life Safety Code and the 2012 Health Care Facilities Code (the “Fire Safety
Requirements”). Prior to the New Compliance Date, LTCFs could demonstrate compliance with
the Fire Safety Requirements by achieving a passing score on the 2001 edition of the National Fire
Protection Association’s Fire Safety Evaluation System (the “2001 FSES”). Following the New
Compliance Date, the 2001 FSES was phased out as a tool for demonstrating compliance with the
Fire Safety Requirements, and it was replaced by the 2013 edition of the National Fire Protection
Association’s Fire Safety Evaluation System (the “2013 FSES”).

Following the New Compliance Date, Brentwood Nursing Home did not achieve a passing score
on the 2013 FSES. As a result, Brentwood Nursing Home obtained the LSC Waiver and engaged
a consultant to recommend steps for achieving a passing score on the 2013 FSES so as to
demonstrate compliance with the Fire Safety Requirements. Brentwood Nursing Home has
followed the recommendations of the consultant, and the consultant has submitted a letter to CMS
indicting that Brentwood Nursing Home is presently in compliance with the Fire Safety
Requirements. It is expected that CMS will promptly issue a letter that Brentwood Nursing Home
is in compliance with the Fire Safety Requirements.

===== PAGE 158 =====
[OCR]
Schedule 18.1.ii

- Brentwood Nursing Home 401(k) Plan

===== PAGE 159 =====
[OCR]
Schedule 18.w.i

As a result of an on-site investigation, the Rhode Island Department of Health (“RIDOH”) on May
21, 2021 issued an Immediate Jeopardy citation. Sunny View Nursing Home (“Sunny View”’
submitted an immediate plan and on May 24, 2021 the Immediate Jeopardy was removed. On May
26, 2021 Sunny View received the 2567 deficiency tags F688, S/S J (free of accident
hazards/supervision devices) and F726 S/S D (competent nursing staff). On June 9, 2021 Sunny
View submitted its Plan of Correction and request for IDR review. On June 10, 2021, the Centers
for Medicare & Medicaid Services imposed a Civil Money Penalty (“CMP”) in the amount of
$104,335. Sunny View’s IDR request was denied and Sunny View will appeal the deficiency tags
and CMP. The appeal is due by August 9, 2021. Sunny View believes there are strong grounds to
remove the deficiency tags and CMP. Sunny View had appropriate patient handling policies in
accordance with 42 CFR § 483.25 and the Immediate Jeopardy is unwarranted, as the resident’s
transfer did not cause serious harm to the resident. At all times Sunny View provided the requisite
standard of care and followed its policies and procedures.

AMENDMENT TO OPERATIONS TRANSFER AGREEMENT

This AMENDMENT TO OPERATIONS TRANSFER AGREEMENT is made as of the 3"
day of November, 2021, by and among the following entities, as current operators: (i)
SUNNY VIEW NURSING HOME, INC., a Rhode Island corporation, (ii) BRENTWOOD
HEALTH CENTER, LTD., a Rhode Island corporation, (iii) BRENTWOOD BY THE
BAY, LTD., a Rhode Island corporation (collectively, (i) through (iii), the “Current
Operators”, and each, a “Current Operator’); and the following entities, as new
operators: (iv) 4000 POST ROAD EDEN OPERATIONS LLC, a Rhode Island limited
liability company, {(v) 83 CORONA STREET EDEN OPERATIONS LLC, a Rhode Island
limited liability company, and (vi) 4040 POST ROAD EDEN OPERATIONS LLC, a
Rhode Island limited liability company (collectively, (iv) through (vi), the “New
Operators”, and each, a “New Operator’).

Recitals

On or about August 4, 2021, Current Operators and New Operators entered into that
certain Operations Transfer Agreement (the “Operations Agreement’), pursuant to
which Current Operators agreed to make an orderly transfer of operations at the
Facilities (as defined in the Operations Agreement) to New Operators, and New
Operators agreed to accept such transfer. Contemporaneously therewith, certain
affiliates of Current Operators (collectively, the “Seller’) entered into an Asset Purchase
Agreement (the “Asset Agreement’) with certain affiliates of New Operators
(collectively, the “Purchaser’), pursuant to which the Seller agreed to sell, and the
Purchaser agreed to purchase, the Purchased Assets (as defined in the Asset
Agreement). Contemporaneously herewith, the Seller and the Purchaser are entering
into an amendment to the Asset Agreement (the “APA Amendment”) to provide that
the Closing Date under the Asset Agreement will be on or before December 31, 2021.
Pursuant to Section 1 of the Operations Agreement, the Commencement Date under
the Operations Agreement was designated as the Closing Date as defined in the Asset
Agreement. Notwithstanding that the Closing Date as defined in the Asset Agreement
(as amended by the APA Amendment) shall occur on or before December 31, 2021, the
Current Operators and the New Operators desire to fix the Commencement Date under
the Operations Agreement as the date following the receipt by New Operators and/or
Purchaser of Final Licensing Approval (as defined below) and the Required
Authorizations (as defined in the Asset Agreement), upon and subject to the conditions
set forth herein.

Current Operators and New Operators, therefore, mutually agree as follows:

Agreements

1. Terms. Capitalized terms used in this Amendment to Operations Transfer
Agreement and not otherwise defined herein shall have the meanings ascribed to them
in the Operations Agreement.

===== PAGE 165 =====
[OCR]
2. Commencement Date. Notwithstanding anything contained in the Operations
Agreement which provides that the Commencement Date shall be the Closing Date as
defined in the Asset Agreement, the Current Operators and the New Operators hereby
agree that the Commencement Date under the Operations Agreement shall be the date
on which New Operators and/or Purchaser receive Final Licensing Approval from the
Rhode Island Department of Health ("RIDOH”), including, without limitation, the License
Approval (as defined in the Operations Agreement) and the Required Authorizations (as
defined in the Asset Agreement). Final Licensing Approval shall occur on the date which
is ten (10) days following the date on which the 30-day appeal period associated with
RIDOH’s approval of the Change in Effective Control Application for the Facilities (as
defined in the Operations Agreement), has lapsed without appeal.

3. Management Agreement. Prior to the Closing Date (as defined in the APA
Amendment), the Current Operators will enter into a management agreement (the
"Management Agreement”), pursuant to which an affiliate of the New Operators (the
“Manager") will manage the Facilities (as defined in the Operations Agreement) from
and after January 1, 2022, through the Commencement Date under the Operations
Agreement. The Management Agreement will reflect terms and conditions reasonably
acceptable to both the Current Operators and the Manager; provided, however, that the
Management Agreement will provide that the Current Operators retain the sole and
exclusive authority at all times to: (i) hire or fire the chief executive officer of the
Facilities; (ii) maintain and control the books and records of the Facilities; (iii) dispose of
assets and incur liabilities on behalf of the Facilities; or (iv) adopt and enforce policies
regarding operation of the Facilities.

4, Ratification. Except as herein modified, the Operations Agreement is hereby
ratified, confirmed, and approved in all respects.

===== PAGE 171 =====
                                 Sunny View Nursing Home, Inc.

                                        Charity Care Policy

1.      Purpose. It shall be the policy of Sunny View Nursing Home, Inc.,
("Provider") to provide free care to certain Qualifying Patients as outlined herein, in Provider's
discretion.

2.      Qualifying Patients. Provider may make free care available to those patients otherwise
medically eligible for Provider's services that also meet the charity care guidelines as outlined
herein ("Qualifying Patients"). Accordingly, Qualifying Patients will be those uninsured patients
with family incomes up to 125% of the Federal Poverty Limits and limited assets. Qualifying
Patients must have applied for any governmental assistance programs for which they qualify. If a
Qualifying Patient is not eligible for governmental assistance or is denied governmental
assistance, such patient may apply for charity care.

3.      Charity Care Policy. Provider agrees it will neither bill Qualifying Patients for services
directly supplied by Provider nor collect any fees for provision of such services. Services that are
provided under contract with an outside provider shall not be eligible for charity care and will be
billed in the customary manner.

4.     Monitoring. An annual summary of charity care will be drafted and provided to the
Provider's management on an annual basis.
