Title: Quality Assessment and Performance Improvement Plan

Policy: To outline a standard format for Quality Assessment and Performance Improvement
which is proactive and continually improves the way the facility cares for and engages residents,
caregivers and other partners. All employees will participate in ongoing improvement activities
in support of the performance improvement mission.

Guiding Principles:

1. Quality Assessment/Performance Improvement (QAPI) has a prominent role in our
management functions.

2. Our organization uses QAPI to make decisions and guide day to day operations.

The outcome of our QAPI program is to improve the quality of life and quality of care for

our residents.

4. QAPI includes all departments, all employees and all services.

QAPI focuses on systems and processes, rather than individuals.

6. Our organization makes decisions based on data. We set goals for performance and
measure our progress.

7. Our culture supports, rather than punishes employees who identify errors.

ed

ms

Design/Scope:

QAPI is ongoing and comprehensive. Activities are directed by the QAPI committee. All
systems of care will be addressed in the program, with a focus on clinical care, quality of life and
resident choice. Safety and quality are a key element of all activities. Evidenced based practice
will be researched and incorporated.

Governance and Leadership:

QAPI activities. Leadership will assure that adequate resources are dedicated to the QAPI
activities. The Quality Assurance Coordinator is designated overall responsibility for the plan
and activities. Training will be provided to staff regarding the QAPI plan, performance
improvement activities and tools. Staff will have time, equipment and technical training needed
to participate in activities. Leadership will assure that QAPI is a priority and assist in setting
expectations around quality, safety, rights and choices. Leadership is responsible for creating an
atmosphere where staff are comfortable identifying and reporting quality problems and
opportunities for improvement.

Facility Administration will develop a Fa that involves staff, resident and family input into

Feedback, Data Systems and Monitoring:

Data will drive all performance tngreyenent activities. Monitoring will occur in each
department to track compliance, identify improvement opportunities and assure that the facility
is meeting or exceeding standards of care practice. Feedback will be obtained from staff,
residents, families and vendors. Performance Indicators will be selected for monitoring each
year. They will be evaluated annually, Results will be analyzed and trended against benchmarks
both internal and external. Goals for improvement will be developed and tracked. Tracking and
investigating outlying areas will be accomplished utilizing proven Quality Assurance

===== PAGE 175 =====
[OCR]
methodologies, i.e. Event flow diagrams, root cause analysis etc. Plans will be developed to
address Adverse Events. The facility utilizes the plan/do/check/act methodology.

’ Performance Improvement Projects:

A performance Improvement Project (PIP) is a concentrated effort in a problem area. It will
involve chartering a team, gathering information, clarifying the issue and proposing solutions.
The QA Coordinator is responsible for chartering the teams and assisting them in the process.
All teams will report results thru the QAPI Committee. The facility conducts PIPs to examine
and improve care or services in areas that the facility identifies as needing attention.

System Analysis and Action:

The facility utilizes a systematic approach to determine when an in-depth analysis is needed to
fully understand a problem, its cause and the implications of change. A QAPI Self-Assessment
will be performed annually to determine the progress with QAPI initiatives. A Performance
Improvement Criteria Checklist will be used to determine if a PIP will be chartered. Once the
decision to charter a team is made, the QA Coordinator will identify a team leader for the process
and assist in selecting members and working with the team throughout the process. Policies and
procedures will be developed to outline the process for Root Cause Analysis and Annual Review
of the QAPI plan. All systems will eventually be comprehensively reviewed in an effort to
prevent future adverse events and promote sustained improvement.

Quality Assurance Performance Improvement Committee
The QAPI Committee members consist at minimum of:
Administrator
Medical Director
Director of Nursing
Quality Assurance Coordinator
Staff Development
Department Heads
Vendors (including pharmacy)
Front Line Staff representing key departments
The purpose of the committee is to lead the facilities QAPI activities, to identify issues related to

care and services and develop, implement and evaluate plans of action to maintain the quality
and safety of care and to correct identified deficiencies.

The Committee processes will be data driven, and may include monitoring results from:

===== PAGE 176 =====
[OCR]
QI/QM report

Nursing Home Compare

Casper Reports

Quality Insights Initiatives

Plan of Correction 01

Resident and Responsible Party Satisfaction Surveys
Chart Audits

Resident Grievances/Concerns
Fall/Restraint/Wound monitoring
Medication Errors
Interdisciplinary audits

Incident Reports

The Committee exists to assure systems are functioning appropriately, to prevent deviation from
care processes and to prevent or correct inappropriate care. The root cause of significant
problems/issues will be identified.

Results of monitoring may lead to:

Policy Revisions or additions
Implementation of new preiea

Staff training/competency development
Physical plant changes/equipment purchase
Root Cause Analysis of Incident

Minutes will be kept on file.

Title: Root Cause Analysis

Policy: To standardize the format and process for conducting a root cause analysis.

Procedure:

L.

2.

~

10.

A root cause analysis (RCA) will be conducted for any sentinel event. In addition,
department heads may initiate a RCA in any area they deem appropriate.

The definition of a sentinel event is:

Accident that results in death or serious injury of staff, resident or visitor.
Elopement

Fire/Major utility failure

Incident with potential for serious harm (re: resident left at outside appointment
unattended)

e. Any incident the Administrator feels warrants a thorough investigation.

f. Employee injuries which result in lost time.

The Administrator will decide if an incident meets the above definitions. All incidents
that are being investigated via root cause analysis will be forwarded to the Quality
Assurance Coordinator. The QAC will assist with the RCA.

The QAC will appoint a team leader and at least three staff to complete the root cause
investigation. Members of the team will include staff that have direct knowledge of the
process involved with the incident.

The team will be given access to all written information related to the accident/incident
(i.e. witness statements, incidents, reports etc.). They may decide to personally interview
staff members involved.

A cause and effect tool will be used to analyze the incident.

At the conclusion of the review, an analysis of the RCA will be documented in
confidential minutes, signed by the administrator and kept on file in the QAC office.
The team leader will mark the documents included in the investigation
“CONFIDENTIAL: FOR USE BY QUALITY ASSURANCE COMMITTEE ONLY”.
The team will submit documentation and discuss results with the Administrator.

The QAC will assign follow-up|responsibility, assure completion and filed documents.
Reports will be generated to Committees as needed (i.e. Safety) All RCAs will be
reported through the Quality Assurance Committee.

RCA should usually be completed within 2 weeks of an incident. The Administrator may
grant an extension as needed. Submit completed RCA to the QAC.

ao Ff

Care Planning Process

Policy:

The resident-centered, goal oriented care plan process is to be interdisciplinary, realistic,
individualized and utilized on a daily basis to promote quality of care and quality of life for all
residents. The following four-step process is to be used in developing care plans that can assist
staff in helping residents achieve their highest practicable physical, mental and psychosocial
well-being, consistent with resident rights. The steps are:

1. Assessment (Problem, Need, Concern Identified)
2. Planning (Goal Setting)

3. Implementation (Developing Approaches)

4, Evaluation (Evaluating Progress Towards Goal)

The Plan of Care must include measurable objectives and timeframes and describe:

¢ Services to be furnished for resident to achieve their highest practicable physical, mental
and psychosocial well-being.

¢ Any services that would otherwise be required but not provided due to the residents

exercise of rights, including the right to refuse treatment.

Any Specialized Services or specialized rehab based on the PASARR recommendations.

In consult with resident, their:

Goals for admission and desired outcomes

Preferences and potential for future discharge. Facility will address residents’ goals for

returning to the community, along with any referrals made for this purpose.

e Discharge Plans

Assessment

1. The team, as per guidelines will complete a comprehensive interdisciplinary assessment
using the Resident Assessment Instrument specified by CMS along with the following
assessments:

Nursing admission assessment

Nutritional Assessment |

Social Services Assessment)

Discharge Planning Review

Activities Assessment

Rehabilitation Screen/Evaluation

Fall Risk Assessment

AIMS

Psychosocial Assessment

Self-Administration of medication assessment

Wander Risk Assessment

===== PAGE 179 =====
[OCR]
e Smoking Assessment
e Risk Indicator for Behaviors
e Continence Assessment

The facility will document who participated in the assessment. Participation must include direct
communication with the resident and direct care staff. The resident assessments will be
coordinated with the PASARR. The facility will refer all Level II residents and all newly
evident or possible mental disorder, intellectual disability, or related conditions for a level II
review upon a significant change is status assessment. The facility will incorporate
recommendations from the PASARR Level II and PASARR evaluation into the resident’s
assessment, care planning and transitions of care.

2:

The Interdisciplinary Team is to include, but not limited to, The Nurse Manager or

designee, Social Worker, Dietitian, Activities Coordinator, Rehabilitation Representative,

Restorative Nursing Representative, Certified Nursing Assistant, and Resident Family

Representative.

Items triggered on the MDS will have corresponding CAA’s completed.

When a resident experiences a ‘change of status” (refer to the MDS user manual), A new

MDS, CAA’s and revised care plan will be developed.

After an assessment has been completed, each problem is identified, and staff needs to

continue the assessment process until the determined why it is a problem for the resident

or those that provide care to the resident.

The care plan process will specifically address, but is not limited to, the following:

e Resident needs, i.e., functional, medical, psychosocial, spiritual, nutritional, social
and educational.

e Advanced Directive Issues, i.e. unable to make informed decisions on admission to
provide more information.

An interim care plan will be developed within 48 hours of admission, utilizing the

following, but not limited to:

e Nursing Admission Assessment

e Risk Assessments

Care Plans will be updated as new needs/problems/concerns arise, and formally reviewed

by the Interdisciplinary team at least quarterly.

All team members involved in the assessment process need to be sure to assess any

educational needs of the resident and/or family and address on the care plan as needed.

Planning (Goal Setting)

1.

After the need/Concern/Problem list has been developed, goals must be set for each
need/concern/problem, which was identified. Goals must be individualized, measureable,
and attainable and have a time frame. Needs identified from triggered CAAs during the
comprehensive MDS process will be reviewed and care planned as resident status
indicates.

All team members, including the resident and/or family representative, participate in
developing the resident centered goals. Residents and/or Family representative/guardians
will be notified at least two weeks in advance of the care plan meeting date. The

===== PAGE 180 =====
[OCR]
exception to this will be in the incidence of a significant change in status and initial care
plan, but all efforts will be made to offer advanced notice of the meeting as allowed.
The resident will have the right to request meetings, along with the right to request
revisions to the plan of care. The resident has the right to participate in establishing the
expected goals and outcomes of the plan of care along with the type, amount, frequency,
duration of care, and any factors related to the effectiveness off the plan of care.

Implementation

L

Individualized Approaches/Interventions are to be developed that assist the resident to
achieve the stated goals.

2. The Care Plan interventions/approaches are to state the person/discipline responsible for

carrying out the intervention/approach.

Evaluation

1.

Although evaluation is a constant process for all identified goals, a formal review process
must take place at least quarterly. Social Services is responsible for notifying the resident
and responsible party regarding the Care Plan reviews.

Documentation of the care plan meeting is to include, but not limited to, the resident
and/or family representatives’ participation in the process and those attending the care
plan meeting.

Care Plans are placed on a regular schedule with changes being made in conjunction with
the MDS dates. Schedules are distributed monthly at a convenient date so that written
reviews can be completed and notifications can be made in a timely manner.

Other Care Planning Guidelines

1.

2.

The care plan is designed as a tool that is used daily to guide the plan of care for each
resident.

As new problems/concerns occur, the care plan is to be updated as soon as possible. The
Licensed Nurse noting new orders that are relevant to the care plan is responsible for
updating the care plan.

All staff involved in any way with completing resident care must have access to and be
familiar with, the resident care plan.
