SUNNY VIEW NURSING HOME, INC.

NOTES TO FINANCIAL STATEMENTS
December 31, 2019 and 2018

NOTE A: NATURE OF BUSINESS

Sunny View Nursing Home, Inc. (the Company) operates a 57 bed nursing home facility
located in Warwick, Rhode Island. The Company provides a full range of nursing home
care primarily to Medicaid, Medicare, and private pay patients in the State of Rhode
Island.

NOTE B: SIGNIFICANT ACCOUNTING POLICIES

DATE OF MANAGEMENT’S REVIEW
Management has evaluated subsequent events through May 29, 2020 the date on
which the financial statements were available to be issued.

CASH AND CASH EQUIVALENTS
For purposes of the statements of cash flows, the Company considers all highly liquid
debt instruments purchased with a maturity of three months or less to be cash
equivalents.

ACCOUNTS RECEIVABLE/UNCOLLECTIBLE ACCOUNTS
The Company carries its accounts receivable at cost less an allowance for doubtful
accounts of $27,000 at December 31, 2019 and 2018. The Company provides for
uncollectible accounts using the direct write-off method for income tax reporting
purposes. Management considers the accounts receivable to be collectible in full.

FIXED ASSETS
Fixed assets are stated at cost. Repairs and maintenance, including replacement of
minor items of physical properties, are charged to current operations while major
additions are capitalized.

DEPRECIATION
Depreciation, for financial reporting purposes, is calculated using the straight-line
method over the estimated service lives of the respective classes of assets. Estimated
lives are as follows:

Leasehold improvements 5-20 years
Furniture and fixtures 5-10 years
Equipment 5-10 years
Automotive equipment 4 years

For income tax reporting purposes, depreciation on fixed assets placed in service after
1980 is calculated using accelerated methods.

LEASES
The Company charges payments under its operating leases to current operations while
leases which are in essence financing arrangements are capitalized and treated as
installment purchases.

See INDEPENDENT ACCOUNTANTS’ REVIEW REPORT

NOTE B: SIGNIFICANT ACCOUNTING POLICIES (CONTINUED)

ESTIMATES
The preparation of financial statements in conformity with generally accepted
accounting principles requires management to make estimates and assumptions that
affect the reported amounts of assets and liabilities and disclosure of contingent assets
and liabilities at the date of the financial statements and the reported amounts of
revenues and expenses during the reporting period. Actual results could differ from
those estimates.

NOTE C: PREPAID EXPENSES
Prepaid expenses consist primarily of unexpired insurance.
NOTE D: LINE-OF-CREDIT

The Company has a $100,000 line-of-credit which bears interest at 0.5% above the prime
interest rate. The line-of-credit is guaranteed by the Company’s stockholders and is
secured by all of the Company’s assets. The loan agreement contains certain restrictive
covenants. Outstanding balance on the line-of-credit was $0 at December 31, 2019 and
2018 respectively.

NOTE E: INCOME TAXES

The Company, with the consent of its stockholders, elected under the Internal Revenue
Code to be an S corporation. In lieu of corporation income taxes, the stockholders of an
S corporation are taxed on their proportionate share of the Company's taxable income.
Therefore, no provision or liability for income taxes has been included in the financial
statements.

The federal income tax returns of the S Corporation for 2016-2019 are subject to
examination by the Internal Revenue Service and State of Rhode Island, generally for the
three years after they were filed.

NOTE F: RETIREMENT PLANS

The Company has a money purchase pension plan for the benefit of employees who meet
minimum age and length of service requirements. Contributions to the plan were
$169,174 and $155,621 for 2019 and 2018, respectively. The Company also has a 401(k)
Profit-Sharing Plan. The Company did not make any contributions to the 401(k) Profit-
Sharing Plan in 2019 or 2018.

See INDEPENDENT ACCOUNTANTS’ REVIEW REPORT
-3-

SUNNY VIEW NURSING HOME, INC.

NOTES TO FINANCIAL STATEMENTS
December 31, 2019 and 2018
(continued)

NOTE G: GUARANTEE

The Company has guaranteed payment on a note between a bank and a related Company.
The balance on the note was $1,695,261 as of December 31, 2019. The agreement
contains certain restrictive covenants and substantially all of the Company's assets serve
as collateral for the note.

NOTE H: RELATED PARTY TRANSACTIONS

LEASE

The Company is obligated under a lease dated August 1, 2012 for the land and
building used in its operations. The current lease expires on July 31, 2022. The lease
is a net lease whereby the lessee is required to pay all expenses relative to the land
and building. Rental expenses were $180,000 and $180,000 for 2019 and 2018,
respectively. The lease has three options to renew for five additional years each. The
minimum future lease payments are as follows: 2020 - $180,000; 2021 - $180,000;
and 2022 - $105,000.

The Company also leases additional space from related parties on a month-to-month
basis. Rent expense was $6,000 and $0 for 2019 and 2018 respectively.

PROMISSORY NOTE
The Company loaned a related party $75,000 for the purpose of investment property.
The loan is for a term of 5 years bearing interest at 5% and is secured by a mortgage.

NOTE I: CONCENTRATIONS OF CREDIT RISK

Financial instruments which potentially subject the Company to concentrations of credit
risk consist principally of accounts receivable and cash in bank. The majority of the
Company's receivables are due from the State of Rhode Island Medicaid Program and
Federal Medicare Program. The Company’s balances in the banks are insured by the
Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation (FDIC) up to $250,000 in each bank. As of
December 31, 2019, the uninsured portion of the balances held in banks was
approximately $581,000. Management does not believe any significant credit risk exists
as of December 31, 2019.

NOTE J: OTHER CURRENT LIABILITIES

Other current liabilities consist primarily of real estate and provider taxes.

See INDEPENDENT ACCOUNTANTS’ REVIEW REPORT

NOTE K: SUBSEQUENT EVENT

The Company’s operations may be affected by the recent and ongoing outbreak of the
coronavirus disease 2019 (COVID-19) which was declared a pandemic by the World
Health Organization in March 2020. The ultimate disruption, which may be caused by the
outbreak, is uncertain; however, it may result in a material adverse impact on the
Company’s financial position, operations and cash flows. Possible effects may include,
but are not limited to, disruption to the Company’s patients and revenue, absenteeism in
the Company’s labor workforce, higher costs, and decline in value of assets held by the
Company.

CHAPUT & FEENEY, LLP

Certified Public Accountants * Business Consultants
INDEPENDENT ACCOUNTANTS’ REVIEW REPORT

To Management
Sunny View Nursing Home, Inc.
Warwick, Rhode Island

We have reviewed the accompanying balance sheet of Sunny View Nursing Home, Inc. (an S
Corporation) , herein after referred to as the “Company”, which comprise the balance sheets as
of December 31, 2020 and 2019, and the related statements of income and retained earnings and
cash flows for the years then ended, and the related notes to the financial statements. A review
includes primarily applying analytical procedures to management's financial data and making
inquiries of company management. A review is substantially less in scope than an audit, the
objective of which is the expression of an opinion regarding the financial statements as a whole.
Accordingly, we do not express such an opinion.

Managements’ Responsibility for the Financial Statements

Management is responsible for the preparation and fair presentation of these financial statements
in accordance with accounting principles generally accepted in the United States of America;
this includes the design, implementation, and maintenance of the internal control relevant to the
preparation and fair presentation of financial statements that are free from material misstatement
whether due to fraud or error.

Accountants’ Responsibility

Our responsibility is to conduct the review in accordance with Statements on Standards for
Accounting and Review Services promulgated by the Accounting and Review Services
Committee of the AICPA. Those standards require us to perform procedures to obtain limited
assurance as a basis for reporting whether we are aware of any material modifications that
should be made to the financial statements for them to be in accordance with accounting
principles generally accepted in the United States of America. We believe that the results of our
procedures provide a reasonable basis for our conclusion.

Accountants’ Conclusion

Based on our reviews, we are not aware of any material modifications that should be made to the
accompanying financial statements in order for them to be in accordance with accounting
principles generally accepted in the United States of America.

Supplementary Information

The supplementary information included in Schedule | is presented for purposes of additional
analysis and is not a required part of the basic financial statements. The information is the
representation of management. We have not audited or reviewed such information and,
accordingly, we do not express an opinion, a conclusion, or provide any assurance on it.

SUNNY VIEW NURSING HOME, INC.

NOTES TO FINANCIAL STATEMENTS
December 31, 2020 and 2019

NOTE A: NATURE OF BUSINESS

Sunny View Nursing Home, Inc. (the Company) operates a 57 bed nursing home facility
located in Warwick, Rhode Island. The Company provides a full range of nursing home
care primarily to Medicaid, Medicare, and private pay patients in the State of Rhode
Island.

NOTE B: SIGNIFICANT ACCOUNTING POLICIES

DATE OF MANAGEMENT’S REVIEW
Management has evaluated subsequent events through May 28, 2021, the date on
which the financial statements were available to be issued.

CASH AND CASH EQUIVALENTS
For purposes of the statements of cash flows, the Company considers all highly liquid
debt instruments purchased with a maturity of three months or less to be cash
equivalents.

ACCOUNTS RECEIVABLE/UNCOLLECTIBLE ACCOUNTS
The Company carries its accounts receivable at cost less an allowance for doubtful
accounts of $27,000 at December 31, 2020 and 2019. The Company provides for
uncollectible accounts using the direct write-off method for income tax reporting
purposes. Management considers the net accounts receivable to be collectible in full.

ADVERTISING
The Company expenses all advertising and promotional costs when incurred.
Advertising expense was $7,878 and $16,543 for the years ended December 31, 2020
and 2019, respectively.

DEPRECIATION
Depreciation, for financial reporting purposes, is calculated using the straight-line
method over the estimated service lives of the respective classes of assets. Estimated
lives are as follows:

Leasehold improvements 5-20 years
Furniture and fixtures 5-10 years
Equipment 5-10 years
Automotive equipment 4 years

For income tax reporting purposes, depreciation on fixed assets placed in service after
1980 is calculated using accelerated methods.

FIXED ASSETS
Fixed assets are stated at cost. Repairs and maintenance, including replacement of
minor items of physical properties, are charged to current operations while major
additions are capitalized.

See INDEPENDENT ACCOUNTANTS’ REVIEW REPORT
-2-

SUNNY VIEW NURSING HOME, INC.

NOTES TO FINANCIAL STATEMENTS
December 31, 2020 and 2019
(continued)

NOTE B: SIGNIFICANT ACCOUNTING POLICIES (CONTINUED)

REVENUE RECOGNITION

The Company recognizes revenue when it satisfies a performance obligation by
transferring control over a product or service to a customer. The majority of the
Company’s revenue originates from contracts with customers to provide various
healthcare services. The majority of the Company’s services represent a bundle of
services that are not capable of being distinct and as such, are treated as a single
performance obligation that is satisfied over time as the services are rendered. The
Company’s standard payment terms are balances due within 30 days.

LEASES
The Company charges payments under its operating leases to current operations
while leases which are in essence financing arrangements are capitalized and treated
as installment purchases.

ESTIMATES
The preparation of financial statements in conformity with generally accepted
accounting principles requires management to make estimates and assumptions that
affect the reported amounts of assets and liabilities and disclosure of contingent assets
and liabilities at the date of the financial statements and the reported amounts of
revenues and expenses during the reporting period. Actual results could differ from
those estimates.

NOTE C: PREPAID EXPENSES
Prepaid expenses consist primarily of unexpired insurance.
NOTE D: SHORT-TERM DEBT

The Company has a $100,000 line-of-credit which bears interest at 0.5% above the prime
interest rate. The line-of-credit is guaranteed by the Company’s stockholders and is
secured by all of the Company’s assets. The loan agreement contains certain restrictive
covenants. There was no outstanding balance on the line-of-credit at December 31, 2020
and 2019.

NOTE E: INCOME TAXES

The Company, with the consent of its stockholders, elected under the Internal Revenue
Code to be an S corporation. In lieu of corporation income taxes, the stockholders of an S
corporation are taxed on their proportionate share of the Company's taxable income, and
accordingly, no provision or liability for income taxes has been included in the financial
statements.

See INDEPENDENT ACCOUNTANTS’ REVIEW REPORT

NOTE E: INCOME TAXES (CONTINUED)

The federal income tax returns of the S Corporation for 2018, 2019, and 2020 are subject
to examination by the Internal Revenue Service and State of Rhode Island, generally for
the three years after they were filed.

NOTE F: RETIREMENT PLANS

The Company has a money purchase pension plan for the benefit of employees who meet
minimum age and length of service requirements. Contributions to the plan were
$186,539 and $169,174 for 2020 and 2019, respectively. The Company also has a 401(k)
Profit-Sharing Plan. The Company did not make any contributions to the 401(k) Profit-
Sharing Plan in 2020 or 2019.

NOTE G: RELATED PARTY TRANSACTIONS

LEASE

The Company is obligated under a lease dated August 1, 2012 for the land and
building used in its operations. The current lease expires on July 31, 2022. The lease
is a net lease whereby the lessee is required to pay all expenses relative to the land
and building. Rental expenses were $180,000 and $180,000 for 2020 and 2019,
respectively. The lease has three options to renew for five additional years each. The
minimum future lease payments are as follows: 2021 - $180,000; and 2022 -
$105,000.

The Company also leases additional space from related parties on a month-to-month
basis. Rent expense was $0 and $6,000 for 2020 and 2019, respectively.

LOANS RECEIVABLE
The Company has made loans to related parties as follows:

In 2018 $75,000 was loaned to a related party. The loan is for a term of 5 years
bearing interest at 5% and is secured by a mortgage.

The Company has paid various joint expenses on behalf of related parties. The
balance as of December 31, 2020 and 2019 was $23,876 and $7,245, respectively.
The loan is unsecured and currently non-interest bearing.

In 2020 the Company loaned a related party $20,000. The loan is unsecured and
currently non-interest bearing.

NOTE H: GUARANTEE

The Company has guaranteed payment on a note between a bank and a related Company.
The balance on the note was $1,613,203 as of December 31, 2020. The agreement
contains certain restrictive covenants and substantially all of the Company's assets serve
as collateral for the note.

NOTE I: OTHER CURRENT LIABILITIES

Other current liabilities consist primarily of real estate and provider taxes.

NOTE J: CONCENTRATIONS OF CREDIT RISK

Financial instruments which potentially subject the Company to concentrations of credit
risk consist principally of accounts receivable and cash in bank. The majority of the
Company's receivables are due from the State of Rhode Island Medicaid Program and
Federal Medicare Program. The Company’s balances in the banks are insured by the
Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation (FDIC) up to $250,000 in each bank. As of
December 31, 2020, the uninsured portion of the balances held in banks was
approximately $1,289,200. Management does not believe any significant credit risk
exists as of December 31, 2020.

NOTE K: SBA PAYCHECK PROTECTION LOAN

The company received loan proceeds in the amount of $742,000 under the Paycheck
Protection Program (“PPP”) administered by a Small Business Administration (SBA)
approved partner. Established as part of the Coronavirus Aid, Relief and Economic Security
Act (‘CARES Act”), the PPP provides for loans to qualifying businesses in amounts up to
2.5 times the business’s average monthly payroll expenses. PPP loans and accrued interest
are forgivable after a ‘covered period” (8 or 24 weeks) as long as the borrower maintains its
payroll levels and uses the loan proceeds for eligible purposes, including payroll, benefits,
rent, and utilities. The forgiveness amount will be reduced if the borrower terminates
employees or reduces salaries during the covered period. Any unforgiven portion of a PPP
loan is payable over two or five years at an interest rate of 1%, with a deferral of payments
for 10 months after the end of the covered period. The Company intends to use PPP loan
proceeds for purposes consistent with the PPP and apply for forgiveness within 10 months of
the end of the covered period.

The Company believes they will receive forgiveness on the total loan balance and as such,
has reported forgiveness income of $742,000 on the statement of operations and retained
earnings as other income at December 31, 2020.

See INDEPENDENT ACCOUNTANTS’ REVIEW REPORT

NOTE L: GRANT INCOME

The Company received various Federal and State grants totaling $577,670 during the year
ended December 31, 2020 as part of the CARES Act. These included $447,960 from the
Federal Stimulus Grants and $129,710 from the State of RI Grants. This income has been
reported as other income on the statement of operations and retained earnings.

NOTE M: COMPENSATED ABSENCES

The Company provides paid vacation to its employees. Compensation for vacation varies by
employee depending on salary, job classification, length of service and other factors. The
Company has determined estimating the amount of compensation for future absences to be
unrealistic. Therefore, no liability has been recorded in the accompanying financial
statements. The Company’s policy is to recognize the costs of the compensated absences
when actually paid to the employee.

NOTE N: SUPPLEMENTAL DISCLOSURES OF CASH FLOW INFORMATION

The Company paid $400 and $400 of state income taxes during the years ended December
31, 2020 and 2019, respectively.

NOTE O: OTHER DISCLOSURE

The Company’s operations may be affected by the recent and ongoing outbreak of the
Coronavirus Disease 2020 (COVID-19) which was declared a pandemic by the World
Health Organization in March 2020. The ultimate disruption, which may be caused by the
outbreak, is uncertain; however, it may result in a material adverse impact on the
Company’s financial position, operations and cash flows. Possible effects may include,
but are not limited to, disruption to the Company’s patients and revenue, absenteeism in
the Company’s labor workforce, higher costs, and decline in value of assets held by the
Company.

See INDEPENDENT ACCOUNTANTS’ REVIEW REPORT

March 18, 2022


Rhode Island Department of Health
3 Capital Hill
Providence, RI 02908

Re: 83 Corona Street Eden Operations LLC d/b/a Sunny View Nursing Home, Inc.
and 4000 Post Road Eden Operations LLC d/b/a Brentwood Health Center

To Whom It May Concern,

We are submitting this letter in response to the February 16, 2022, deficiency question 6
in connection with the 83 Corona Street Eden Operations LLC and 4000 Post Road
Eden Operations LLC Change in Effective Control Applications (“the Applicants”). Mr.
Louis “Josh” Gellis has 100% ownership interest in the Applicants.

The Applicants’ affiliates have been in the nursing home industry for many years and
have never experienced any failures or defaults. The Applicants’ affiliates also have the
banking and industry relationships needed to ensure success. We have and continue to
prepare tax returns for Mr. Gellis and the Applicants’ affiliates.

principal’s successful operation of skilled nursing facilities, the Applicants have the
financial ability to consummate the purchase of and to safely operate Sunny View
Nursing Home, Inc. and Brentwood Health Center, skilled nursing and other
rehabilitation facilities in Rhode Island.

 ADVANCE RATES:     Availability under the RLOC shall be an amount equal to up to 85% of the “net
                    collectable value” of the billed accounts receivable from third-party payors that are
                    aged less than 150 days from date of service. During the Change of Ownership
                    (“CHOW”) period, not to exceed 120 days, availability shall include unbilled
                    accounts from third party payors ages less than 120 days from date of service. After
                    the CHOW period, unbilled availability shall be no greater than 30 days.

                    The “net collectable value” of RLOC Borrower’s accounts receivable is the amount
                    RLOC Borrower bills third-party payors less deductible obligations, contractual
                    allowances, and reserves which availability shall be determined by Lender upon
                    completion of its on-site due diligence and which shall be set forth in applicable
                    loan documents to be provided to RLOC Borrower.

                    STL: A first-priority lien and security interest in all existing and future assets of STL
